No `storage.objects` policies added; uploads/reads go through service-role server code only, with the BPLO-role check enforced in the calling server action, matching `application-documents`' existing pattern exactly. `lgus.print_template_path`/`print_template_field_mapping` (same migration) store at most one active template per LGU -- a template is inherently a singleton per LGU, same shape as `automated_assessment_enabled`, not a new table.

**Filling and serving** (`/api/dashboard/print-permit`, updated): tries the LGU's own uploaded template first if one exists, and falls back to the generated certificate -- not just when no template is set, but also if filling the custom one throws for *any* reason (a corrupted stored file, a field renamed since the mapping was saved). BPLO must always get something printable; a broken custom template must never block the workflow, same reasoning `permit-pdf.ts`'s own try/catch around PDF/QR generation at signing already established (7k). `fillCustomTemplate` (`print-template-fill.ts`) skips a mapped field that no longer exists or isn't a text type rather than failing the whole document, and leaves an unmapped or empty-valued field completely untouched (so the template's own printed label/placeholder for that field stays visible, not blanked).

**Verified two ways, not just type-checked**: constructed a real fillable PDF programmatically (a colored background + three named text fields + a checkbox, simulating what a BPLO-commissioned design would actually look like) and ran it through the real `inspectPdfFields`/`validateMapping`/`fillCustomTemplate` functions -- confirmed the checkbox is correctly excluded from fillable options, an invalid mapping is correctly rejected, and (rendering the actual output PDF and looking at it, not just checking exit codes) the custom background/title text came through completely untouched while the three mapped fields filled in with the exact right formatted values and the unmapped checkbox stayed empty. Also applied migration 0034 directly to production and confirmed both new `lgus` columns and the new storage bucket exist.

---

## 7z. A reusable prompt for converting an LGU's Revenue Code into 7s's CSV import (2026-08-15, same day)

The project owner asked to turn the ad-hoc process 7s's own onboarding relies on (someone reads the LGU's real ordinance and fills out the CSV templates) into something repeatable -- a documented process plus a copy-pasteable prompt, so future onboarding doesn't depend on re-deriving this from scratch in a fresh conversation each time.

**The process**: get the actual primary source (whatever format -- Excel, Word, PDF, a scan), preferring the real signed ordinance over any staff-prepared summary spreadsheet when both exist (the same lesson 7b already paid for once with San Miguel's own onboarding); classify each fee type's shape (LBT is almost always graduated brackets, but check for a "new business = X% of capital investment, floored at the schedule minimum" special provision; Mayor's Permit Fee is either a named-business-type catalog or a business-size tier grid -- CLAUDE.md 7r's two known shapes, flag a third if one ever shows up); convert to the exact CSV column format; upload and read the plain-language preview against the source one more time before publishing.

**The prompt itself embeds the full column schema** (both `LBT_HEADERS` and `MP_HEADERS`, with every column's meaning spelled out) directly, rather than pointing at the codebase -- deliberately, so it works standalone in a fresh conversation with no repository context, not just inside Claude Code. Its one non-negotiable instruction, stated first and reinforced at the end: never guess or estimate a rate, bracket boundary, legal citation, or exemption -- flag anything ambiguous, contradictory, illegible, or missing instead. A `KNOWN GAPS` section lists the shapes 7s's CSV format genuinely can't express yet (flat per-unit catalogs with no basis figure, a Mayor's Permit fallback tiered by prior-year LBT paid, anything conditioned on a detail the applicant form doesn't capture) so a future run flags those explicitly rather than forcing a bad fit.

**First real use immediately validated the process and caught a real parser bug** (see 7s's own same-day follow-up for the fix): run against a fresh 17-page scan of San Miguel's actual ordinance (different pages than the original scan 7b worked from), the prompt correctly flagged a province ambiguity (five Philippine municipalities are named San Miguel, and these particular pages never state which one), missing preliminary pages, two internally-inconsistent numbers in the scan (a rate and a bracket total that don't reconcile against the surrounding math), and independently rediscovered the exact same real ₱150/₱250/₱350 Mayor's Permit prior-year-LBT tiering already documented in 7g -- a genuine cross-check that the original hand-built seed data was accurate. The resulting CSV, built exactly as the prompt instructed, then failed to upload -- not because the conversion was wrong, but because `parseLbtCsv`'s own validation was stricter than what the prompt (accurately) told the user to produce.

## 7aa. Engineering-assessed Building Permit Fee (2026-08-15, same day)

The project owner asked for a new fee source that didn't fit anything already built: "the fee that the Engineering Department apply to the applicant." Researched before designing anything, per this project's own standing rule about not guessing a real-world specific: it's the **Building Permit Fee**, governed by the National Building Code (PD 1096), assessed by the Office of the Building Official (in most municipalities, the Municipal Engineer's Office -- matching this LGU's "Engineering" department exactly). Confirmed genuinely variable, not flat -- computed per square meter of floor area, construction cost, and occupancy type from the actual submitted building plans, which nothing on the applicant form captures. That's why neither existing fee mechanism fits: `fee-engine.ts` can't compute a number it has no input data for, and the flat Regulatory Fees mechanism (Settings) only handles a single amount that's the same for every application. The project owner's own instinct -- Engineering types in their own already-computed figure, right at the point of review -- was the correct shape for how this fee actually works, not a workaround.

One naming nuance surfaced during research and flagged directly rather than assumed away: this project's own `reference/unreconciled/muniserve_new.docx` separately mentions a flat "Building Inspection Fee" (₱300) -- a different, already-solvable case (the existing Regulatory Fees mechanism) if it's real and distinct from the variable Building Permit Fee. The project owner confirmed the variable one is what's meant.

**Design confirmed directly before building** (a discuss-first pass, matching the project's fee-import and print-template precedents): Engineering-only for now (not a generic per-department fee mechanism), and Approve must be blocked until an amount is entered.

**Schema** (migration `0035_building_permit_fee.sql`): `lgus.building_permit_fee_enabled` (off by default -- no LGU sees the field until BPLO deliberately turns it on) and `lgus.building_permit_fee_label` (BPLO-editable text, not hardcoded "Building Permit Fee", precisely because of the naming ambiguity found above). `department_reviews.assessed_amount` -- captured together with Engineering's own decision, not a detached number, so it's audited exactly like every other department action already is (reviewer, timestamp). No new RLS policy needed for either: `lgus` reuses migration 0027's existing BPLO-update policy, `department_reviews` reuses migration 0002's existing department/BPLO-scoped policies -- both already bound which *rows* a session can touch, not which columns, the same "RLS bounds rows, not columns" convention `setStaffActive`/`setAutomatedAssessmentEnabled` already established.

**The gate lives in two places, matching the LBT-category dead-end precedent (7p)**: the primary guard is the Approve/Approve-with-condition buttons going inert client-side until a value is typed -- genuinely dynamic (has to react to live input, unlike the LBT-category gate's static server-computed condition), so this is the first review surface that needed a real client component. Extracted into a new shared `DepartmentReviewActions` (`src/app/dashboard/department-review-actions.tsx`), used by both the department's own dashboard and BPLO's "act on a department's behalf" inline row -- one component instead of two near-duplicate forms, same reasoning as `AwaitingPaymentSection`. The server-side backstop lives in `submitDepartmentDecision` (`review-workflow.ts`): checked via a separate read before the real write (the department name isn't known from the caller's own params, only from the row), so a stale form can never slip an Engineering approval through without a real amount when the LGU has this on.

**`getEngineeringAssessedAmount`** (`review-workflow.ts`) -- the "latest approved decision across rounds" lookup, same shape `areAllDepartmentsCleared` already needed for the identical reason: Engineering may have approved (and entered their figure) in an earlier round and never been re-triggered since (rule #6), so the real answer isn't necessarily on the most recent round's row. Used by both `AssessmentCard`'s live preview (`bplo/page.tsx`) and `finalizeAssessment`'s actual write (`bplo/actions.ts`) -- the same lookup both places, so the number BPLO sees before finalizing is guaranteed to be the one that actually gets charged, not a second independent guess. Appears as its own `regulatory`-category line, `isManualEligible: false` (Engineering already supplied a real human-entered figure -- it should never become one of the Automated-Assessment-off manual-entry fields BPLO has to separately re-type, the same reasoning CEDULA/discount lines are already exempt from that toggle).

Verified directly against production, not just type-checked: confirmed Engineering's own RLS-scoped session can write `assessed_amount`, confirmed BPLO's session can toggle/label the setting, and replicated `getEngineeringAssessedAmount`'s exact "latest across rounds" query against a real application (MS-2026-00003, approved before this feature existed) -- correctly resolves to no amount rather than erroring, confirming applications that predate this feature aren't affected until an LGU actually turns it on.

---

## 7bb. Click feedback: every button and nav tab was silent while working (2026-08-16)

The project owner's own words: "when I click on anything, it doesn't show anything, it's as if there's nothing happening." True and systemic, not a one-off -- a plain `<form action={serverAction}>` gives zero visual feedback while the action runs, by default, and that's the pattern nearly every button on every dashboard page uses (Approve/Reject, every Settings toggle, Mark as printed/released, Record payment, ...).

**Two separate mechanisms for two separate causes**, not one fix:

- **Form/server-action submits** (the actual majority of "nothing happens" reports): `PrimaryButton`/`OutlineButton`/`MiniButton`/`GhostButton` moved to a new `src/app/dashboard/pending-ui.tsx` ("use client"), rebuilt around React's `useFormStatus()` -- stable, built into `react-dom` since React 19, already this project's installed version, no new dependency. `ui.tsx` re-exports the same four names unchanged, so every existing `import { PrimaryButton, ... } from "../ui"` across the whole dashboard (bplo, department, treasury, mayor, settings, businesses -- everywhere) is fixed at once with zero changes needed at any call site. A button now shows a small spinner and goes inert the instant its form starts submitting, which also incidentally closes a real latent bug: nothing previously stopped a double-click from firing the same server action twice.

  One real nuance worth remembering: `useFormStatus()` reflects the whole enclosing `<form>`'s pending state, not which specific button was clicked -- so `DecisionButtons`' four buttons (Approve/Approve w/ condition/Request info/Reject) all show pending together the instant any one of them is clicked. Confirmed this is a *feature*, not a bug, before shipping it: it means all four go inert together, which is exactly the right behavior (prevents a second click landing a conflicting decision while the first is still in flight), not a cosmetic imprecision to fix.

  `useFormStatus()` returns `{ pending: false }` by default when a button isn't inside a `<form>` at all, or is `type="button"` -- confirmed against React's own docs before relying on this, then confirmed directly: a button with no enclosing form, or explicitly `type="button"`, never shows pending, so this is safe to apply unconditionally everywhere these four components are already used, including the CSV-import wizard's own plain `onClick`-driven buttons.

- **Tab/nav-pill navigation** (`DashboardTopBar`, `BusinessesSubNav`): a new `NavLinkPendingHint` (same file), using `useLinkStatus()` -- confirmed against this project's actual installed Next 16 docs (`node_modules/next/dist/docs`) before using it, not assumed from training data, per AGENTS.md's own standing warning about this specific Next.js version. Deliberately a secondary, subtle touch (a small pulsing dot next to the tab label), not the primary fix -- Next's own docs say outright a route with a `loading.js` file (this dashboard already has one, section 7e) may skip the pending phase entirely on a fast navigation. This just closes the small gap before that skeleton appears, and only costs a few lines since `dashboard/loading.tsx` already does the heavier lifting.

**Verified directly, not just type-checked or assumed from the hook's documented behavior**: built a temporary unauthenticated fixture page (same technique as 7o/7p/7s -- deleted immediately after) with a real server action carrying a 3-second artificial delay, then drove it with actual DOM clicks and polled the real button state at 50ms/300ms/1000ms/3200ms. Confirmed all four submit buttons disable and show a spinner together the instant the form starts submitting, a `type="button"` sibling in the same form never does either, and everything correctly clears the moment the action resolves.

**Follow-up, same day**: the project owner tested this live and reported it still didn't work for tab navigation specifically -- switching Applications → Businesses showed nothing but the existing gray `loading.tsx` skeleton, no nav bar at all in the screenshot. Root cause: `DashboardTopBar` (and therefore `NavLinkPendingHint`'s dot) was rendered inside each individual `page.tsx`'s own returned JSX, not in the persistent `dashboard/layout.tsx`. Next's `loading.tsx` convention replaces the *entire* `{children}` route-segment content during a slow navigation via Suspense -- so the whole previous page, nav bar and pending dot included, unmounted and was swapped for the generic skeleton, which has no nav pills in it at all. The mechanism meant to provide feedback disappeared before it could be perceived, exactly matching what the screenshot showed.

Fix: hoisted `DashboardTopBar` out of all nine dashboard pages (bplo, businesses, businesses/history, department, treasury, mayor, audit, stats, settings) and into `dashboard/layout.tsx`, via a new client component `src/app/dashboard/persistent-top-bar.tsx` that derives the active tab from `usePathname()` (the one prop that varies per page rather than per role, so it can't be computed once server-side the way `officeLabel`/`applicationsHref`/etc. already are). `layout.tsx` renders it above `{children}`, outside the Suspense boundary `loading.tsx` replaces, so it now survives every navigation untouched. The `settingsHref`/`auditHref`/`statsHref` role gating that used to be duplicated per-page (`role === "bplo"`, `role === "bplo" || "mayor"`) is centralized in `layout.tsx` instead -- verified line-by-line against every page's own prior gating logic before doing this, a behavior-preserving move, not a functional change. Also dropped the fake top-bar placeholder rectangle from `loading.tsx`'s skeleton, since the real one no longer unmounts and a placeholder next to the real thing would just double up.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` on every touched file, and a full `npm run build` all clean. Then built a temporary fixture route (`test-topbar-fixture/`, deleted immediately after, same technique as above) reproducing the exact structure -- a layout rendering `PersistentTopBar` above `{children}`, a `loading.tsx` sibling, and dynamic child routes with a 6-second artificial server delay so a cache-miss navigation stays mid-flight long enough to inspect. Confirmed via `read_network_requests` that clicking between routes fires only an RSC fetch (`?_rsc=...`), never a full document reload, and via direct DOM inspection mid-navigation that the top bar's `<nav>` and its links stay mounted (identical accessibility-tree ref IDs before and after) while `loading.tsx`'s skeleton is showing in `{children}` -- the top bar genuinely persists through the Suspense boundary now instead of vanishing with the page it belonged to.

---

## 7cc. Downloadable submitted-form PDF (2026-08-16)

The project owner asked for a way to download exactly what an applicant filled up when they submitted an application -- a non-editable PDF record for staff, format specifically chosen (PDF) so it can't be edited afterward.

**A real architectural gap found before building anything**: the ~40 fields an applicant types (business name, address, TIN, employee counts, lessor info, etc.) don't live on `applications` -- they live on the mutable `businesses` row, which gets overwritten by every later renewal. Only the uploaded documents (already `application_id`-scoped) and two numbers (`form_inputs.capital_investment`/`gross_sales`) are tied to a specific application. Downloading "the form for application X" using current business data would silently show newer data than what was actually submitted, for any application that isn't a business's most recent one -- a real problem for something meant to be a tamper-proof record. Presented this to the project owner directly with two options (reconstruct from current data vs. snapshot at submission) rather than silently picking one; they chose the snapshot approach.

**Migration `0036_application_form_snapshot.sql`**, live in production: `applications.form_snapshot jsonb`, nullable. No RLS change needed (RLS bounds rows on `applications`, already covers this column, per the standing convention).

**Captured at submission time**, two sources:
- `submit-application/route.ts` (the applicant's own online form): snapshots the same `values` object already built for field validation, minus the 9 document-id fields (not human-readable -- the PDF lists actual documents separately, queried live from `documents`) and `phone` (the `"verified"` placeholder set for validation, not a real value -- see 7d/7h's existing reasoning for why phone is read live from `owners` instead).
- `businesses/actions.ts`'s `startWalkInApplication` (BPLO filing on behalf of a walk-in): this flow deliberately doesn't re-collect the ~40-field profile (own doc comment, unchanged), so its snapshot is the business's own current profile at filing time via `mapBusinessProfile()`, with `capitalInvestment`/`grossSales` overridden by the one figure actually being filed -- genuinely accurate to "what was submitted" for a counter transaction, since BPLO is vouching for the existing record, not retyping it.

**`src/lib/application-form-pdf.ts`**: renders the PDF. Section titles and field labels copied directly from `reference/official-application-form/fields.json`'s real `customFieldLabel`/header text (one source typo, "INFORMATON", corrected -- this document doesn't claim pixel-exact facsimile status the way print-certificate.ts's own doc comment already establishes for that certificate). A pre-migration application with no snapshot falls back to reconstructing from the business's current profile + this application's own `form_inputs`, rendered with `snapshot.source === "reconstructed"` triggering a visible disclaimer banner rather than silently presenting possibly-stale data as exact.

**`src/app/api/dashboard/application-form-pdf/route.ts`**: any staff role at the application's own LGU can download (read-only, same audience as the Business Registry itself), not BPLO-only -- matches the "staff only" scope the project owner confirmed (not the applicant).

Download links added wherever staff already review an application's documents (BPLO's initial-review card, a department's review card, next to the existing `DocumentList`) and in the Business Registry's per-business application history, so it's reachable both while reviewing and when looking back at a business's past filings later.

**Verified against real production data, not just type-checked**: a temporary script (deleted after use, same pattern as this project's other one-off verification scripts) called `generateApplicationFormPdf()` directly against San Miguel's real first-ever test application (`MS-2026-00001`, no snapshot -- exercises the reconstructed fallback) and a synthetic full-field payload (exercises the snapshot path), then read the actual rendered PDF bytes back. Caught and fixed two real bugs this way, not found by type-checking:
- Long field labels (e.g. "Do You Have Tax Incentives from Any Government Entity?") overflowed into the value column and visually overlapped it -- the row renderer had no wrapping at all. Fixed with a proper word-wrap on both label and value columns, row height computed from whichever wraps to more lines.
- The reconstructed fallback for `grossSales` read only `form_inputs.gross_sales`, which is `null` on this exact real application (its `form_inputs` predates the current shape entirely -- an older `basis_amount`-keyed version) -- silently dropping a real, known figure (₱3,462,287, still recoverable from the business's own `gross_sales_history`) instead of falling back to it. Fixed to prefer `form_inputs` when present, fall back to the business's current derived value otherwise, only for the two fields (`grossSales`/`capitalInvestment`) where a same-application-year fallback source actually exists.

## 7dd. Business Registry discoverability (2026-08-16)

The project owner reported not seeing anywhere to look up a business and see its full details -- turned out the Business Registry (`/dashboard/businesses`) already does exactly this via a click-to-expand row (`<details>`/`<summary>`), just with a bare chevron icon as the only affordance. Confirmed with the project owner this was a discoverability gap, not a missing feature. Added a visible "View details" label next to the chevron (desktop widths only, matching the existing `dateLabel` column's own `sm:` breakpoint, so mobile rows don't get crowded) so the row reads unambiguously as expandable rather than relying on an icon alone.

## 7ee. Two real gaps in the legacy-claim/renewal identity flow (2026-08-16)

Talking through the previous section surfaced two real, confirmed bugs in how a business gets linked to a real owner account -- both traced through the actual code and production data before touching anything, not assumed.

**Bug 1 -- a legacy claim could silently fail to link on retry.** `verify-otp/route.ts` only ran the "link this legacy business to whoever's verifying" step inside the branch for a brand-new owner (no existing `owners` row for that phone). The "renewal_license" screen's own "Already claimed your business before? Sign in with your phone instead" link (meant for someone's *second* renewal year) has nothing stopping a legacy owner from clicking it on their *first* attempt instead of using License Number lookup. Traced the exact failure: that first attempt creates a bare owner row for their phone with no business attached; the app then correctly redirects them to License Number lookup; but on the retry, the phone now already has an owner row, so the "existing owner" branch runs instead -- and the actual claim logic lived only in the other branch. The business stays `is_legacy_unclaimed` forever, and the applicant hits a confusing "business not found or not yours" error only once they try to submit, disconnected from the real cause. Same root cause blocked an owner with more than one still-unclaimed legacy business from ever claiming a second one via License Number, once their phone already had an owner row from claiming the first.

Fix: the claim step (look up the legacy business, then link it) now runs whenever a valid `legacyBusinessId` was passed, independent of whether the owner row already existed -- resolved before the owner lookup, not nested inside one branch of it. No schema change, no client change needed (the client already assumed the server would claim whenever it sent a `legacyBusinessId` -- that assumption is just true again).

**Bug 2 -- no way back in if a returning owner's phone number stops working.** A phone number is the only credential this app uses (OTP-based login, no password) -- if an owner loses their old number between renewal years (a genuinely common multi-year scenario: lost phone, changed SIM), there was no path back in for them, self-service or staff-assisted, and no code anywhere actually attempted to fix it.

Self-service can't safely fix this by itself: letting License Number lookup re-link an *already-claimed* business to a new phone would mean anyone who's ever seen that number (printed on a permit or a receipt) could hijack someone else's account. License Number alone is only a safe bar for the very first claim, when nobody owns the business yet.

So this ships as two pieces, both reusing this app's existing "staff verifies identity in person" trust model (rule #9's precedent) rather than inventing a new one:
- **`updateOwnerPhone`** (`businesses/actions.ts`), BPLO-only: updates the phone on an already-linked owner directly, after BPLO verifies who they are in person -- exactly the same trust boundary as every other counter transaction in this app already uses. Blocks reassigning to a number already registered to a *different* owner (a real "same person, two records" merge case is rare enough, and `owners.merged_into_owner_id` already exists unused in the schema for exactly that, that it's better left a deliberate future decision than silently allowed here). New UPDATE RLS policy on `owners` (migration `0037`, live in production) -- `owners` never had one before this (only SELECT and BPLO-only INSERT, migration 0002), so even this staff-assisted fix was impossible until now. A small form in the Business Registry's expanded row (BPLO-only, only shown once a business has an owner) shows the masked current number and lets BPLO enter a new one.
- **Clearer self-service messaging, not a bypass**: `lookup-license/route.ts` used to report an already-claimed business identically to "no business with that number exists at all" -- a dead end with no explanation. Now distinguishes the two (`alreadyClaimed: true` + a masked hint of the phone it's under, via a new `maskPhone()` in `src/lib/mask.ts`), so the apply flow can say "this business is already on file under a mobile number ending in ####; if that's no longer your number, visit the BPLO office" instead of leaving them guessing.

**Verified against real production data**, not just read: three rolled-back SQL transactions against the new `owners` UPDATE policy confirmed BPLO can update the phone for an owner linked to a business at their own LGU, cannot update an owner unrelated to their LGU, and a non-BPLO role (department) cannot update even a same-LGU linked owner. The `lookup-license` response changes were exercised directly against the live dev server for all three real cases (never existed, still-unclaimed legacy match, already-claimed match against San Miguel's own real claimed test business) -- confirmed the masked phone hint matches the real owner's actual number, and that the existing unclaimed-match response shape is unchanged.

**Same-day follow-up: a third gap, plus a real bug in already-shipped code found while verifying it.** The project owner pointed out `updateOwnerPhone`'s form only shows once a business already has an owner -- for a still-legacy business, the only way to attach a phone at all was `startWalkInApplication`'s own phone field, bundled with filing a full application (requires an amount, submits a real renewal/new application). Someone who just wants to get set up in the system -- calls in, or stops by without renewing that same visit -- had no way to do just that. Added `claimLegacyBusiness`: same find-or-create-owner-and-link logic as the walk-in form's own phone branch, minus everything else attached to it. Business Registry's expanded row now shows this form instead of the phone-update one whenever a business has no owner yet.

Verifying this against real production data (rolled-back transactions, same technique as above) surfaced a real, currently-live bug -- not in the new code, in the existing, already-shipped `startWalkInApplication`. `owners` has a SELECT policy that only lets staff see an owner already linked to one of their businesses (by design, migration 0002) -- but a brand-new owner isn't linked to anything at the exact moment it's created. Supabase's `.insert({...}).select("id")` is `INSERT ... RETURNING id` at the SQL level, and Postgres raises an RLS violation on RETURNING a row the SELECT policy would exclude, even though the INSERT's own WITH CHECK passed. So `startWalkInApplication`'s "create a new owner for a genuinely new phone number" branch -- the single most common way a legacy business actually gets claimed at the counter -- was silently failing in production the whole time this pattern existed. Confirmed directly: a plain `INSERT` with no `RETURNING` succeeds under the exact same session; adding `RETURNING id` to the identical statement fails with `new row violates row-level security policy for table "owners"`. Fixed both `startWalkInApplication` and the new `claimLegacyBusiness` the same way -- generate the id client-side (`crypto.randomUUID()`), insert it explicitly, never ask for it back. No RLS/schema change needed; standard, safe pattern for a `uuid default gen_random_uuid()` column.

Re-verified end-to-end after the fix: a full rolled-back transaction (insert with explicit id, then the businesses UPDATE, then re-reading the row) confirmed the whole claim flow now completes and the business becomes visible to that BPLO session again once linked.

## 7ff. CEDULA: online vs. counter-paid, per LGU (2026-08-16)

Some LGUs want CEDULA folded into the online assessment total; some want to keep it exactly as it works today (paid separately at the Treasury counter, applicant uploads a copy). Discussed before building anything, since it touched a real design question: `fee-engine.ts` already computed `includedInTotal` for CEDULA directly from `fee_rules.delivery_mode` (`cedulaRule.delivery_mode === "online"`) -- that part was already generic -- but two real gaps meant it never actually worked as a live setting: (1) no Settings UI ever touched `delivery_mode` for CEDULA specifically (it was fixed at import/seed time, and the existing "Regulatory Fee Flat Amounts" section only manages `fee_category = 'regulatory'`), and (2) the application form's CEDULA-upload requirement was hardcoded on unconditionally, with zero connection to `delivery_mode` -- so even if delivery_mode had been flipped by hand, every applicant would've still been forced to upload a document that, in "online" mode, can't possibly exist yet (they're buying the CEDULA *through* this transaction, not proving they already have one).

Confirmed with the project owner before building: CEDULA's own amount is a fixed national formula (RA 7160), never LGU-customizable -- so this is purely a delivery-mode toggle, never a rate editor.

**No new `lgus` column.** `LguDisplay.cedulaIncludedOnline` (`src/lib/lgu.ts`) is computed live from the LGU's own CEDULA `fee_rules` rows (`getCedulaIncludedOnline()`, checking one row -- both are always toggled together, there's no scenario where individual/juridical should disagree on delivery mode) rather than adding a second, potentially-drifting source of truth alongside the column `fee-engine.ts` already reads.

**Settings** (`setCedulaIncludedOnline`, `settings/actions.ts`): BPLO-only, flips `delivery_mode` on both of the LGU's CEDULA rows together (`online` / `reference_only`). New UPDATE-only RLS policy (migration `0038`, live in production) -- `fee_rules` already had per-category write policies for `regulatory` (0027) and `lbt`/`mayors_permit` (0028), but never `cedula`, so BPLO had no write access to these two rows at all before this. Scoped narrower than those two (UPDATE only, not ALL) since this feature never creates/deletes a CEDULA row, only ever flips the one column on the two that are seeded once at onboarding.

**Application form**: `cedulaDoc`'s conditional visibility is handled OUTSIDE `application-form-logic.ts`'s rule engine, deliberately -- that engine is about applicant-*answer*-driven visibility (nature of business, employee counts, etc.), a different concern from an LGU-level setting. `ApplyPageClient.tsx`'s document-list filter and `submit-application/route.ts`'s required-fields check each got one added condition (`!(field === "cedulaDoc" && cedulaIncludedOnline)`) rather than threading LGU state into the pure rule engine. `submit-application/route.ts` had to move its existing `resolveLguId` call earlier (it used to run after the required-fields check) so the flag is known before validation runs, not just before the writes.

**Verified against real production data**: three rolled-back SQL transactions against the new RLS policy (BPLO/own-LGU updates both rows together, BPLO/different-LGU affects zero rows, non-BPLO/own-LGU affects zero rows) and a direct call to `getCedulaIncludedOnline()`/`getLguDisplay()` against San Miguel's real, current `reference_only` CEDULA rows, confirming both correctly resolve to `false` today. Didn't flip San Miguel's real live setting to `online` just to eyeball the form -- that's a real pilot client's actual configuration, and the "online" branch of a one-line boolean filter is adequately covered by code review once the "false" branch is confirmed against real data.

**Same-day follow-up: reference_only CEDULA no longer shown at all, not just excluded from the total.** Flagged live by the project owner against a real San Miguel application in assessment (`MS-2026-00005`): the assessment card was still showing a CEDULA line with its computed amount even though the LGU has it set to counter-paid, matching `fee-engine.ts`'s original design for *any* reference_only line (still shown, with a "paid at the counter, not part of the online total" note, same as a reference_only regulatory fee) -- correct in that the ₱305 was genuinely excluded from the total (verified: total matched the sum of every *other* line exactly), but the project owner's own call was that showing an amount at all next to an otherwise-online breakdown reads as confusing regardless of the footnote, now that CEDULA specifically has a live Settings toggle governing it. Changed `computeApplicationFees()`'s CEDULA block: a `reference_only` CEDULA rule is no longer pushed as a line at all (not merely excluded from the total) -- Treasury computes their own counter amount independently regardless, so nothing downstream depends on this preview existing. Kept the pre-existing "no active CEDULA rule found" warning distinct from this -- a genuinely missing/misconfigured rule is still worth flagging as an onboarding gap; an existing rule that's just set to reference_only is not.

Re-verified directly against the same real application (`MS-2026-00005`, real business/gross-sales data) after the change: no `cedula`-category line in the result at all, total unchanged (still exactly the sum of the other 7 lines), no new warnings introduced.

## 7gg. Renewal lookup: License Number -> Permit Number, OTP to the phone on file (2026-08-16)

Grew directly out of the "does our app generate a license number" conversation: it doesn't, for a business registered fresh through MuniServe -- the only number every business actually gets is a **Permit Number** (`applications.reference_number`, what's printed as "Permit No." on the actual certificate). The old "Renewal via License Number" lookup only ever matched a still-unclaimed legacy business (`is_legacy_unclaimed = true`); an already-claimed business had no lookup-by-number path at all, only "sign in with your own phone" -- which meant a returning owner had to correctly remember which number they originally registered with, or hit the "already registered, visit BPLO" wall added earlier the same day.

Design discussed with the project owner before building. Now: `lookup-license/route.ts` searches BOTH `businesses.legacy_license_no` (an older paper permit) and `applications.reference_number` (any of a business's own past permit numbers -- any one resolves to the same business), for a business regardless of claim status. What happens next branches on whether it's already claimed:

- **Already claimed**: the applicant proves control of the REAL phone already on file, not a new one they type. `lookup-license` only ever hands the client a **masked** hint (`maskPhone()`), never the real number. A confirm step ("This business is registered under a number ending in ●●1374 — send a code there?") sits between the lookup and actually sending anything, both to avoid burning a real Semaphore SMS credit on a mistyped number and to avoid texting a real business owner because someone else's number was close. New route `send-renewal-otp/route.ts` resolves the destination phone server-side from `businessId` and sends there -- the client never learns or transmits the real number at any point. `verify-otp/route.ts` gained a matching `businessId` branch: when present, the phone the OTP is checked against is resolved server-side the same way, so a client-supplied phone (there isn't one in this path) is never trusted. This **replaces** the earlier same-day "already registered, visit BPLO" dead-end entirely -- what used to require a counter visit now resolves itself whenever the applicant still has that phone; BPLO's `updateOwnerPhone` (7ee) is still the fallback for when they don't.
- **Still unclaimed**: unchanged -- confirm, then the applicant supplies a NEW phone to claim it (there's nothing on file to text yet).

Extracted `sendOtpCode()` into `src/lib/otp.ts` (cooldown check + create + SMS send + error handling) so `send-otp/route.ts` (client-supplied phone, the "new business"/"sign in with your phone" paths) and the new `send-renewal-otp/route.ts` share one implementation of the same guarantee (no more than one code per phone per 30 seconds) instead of two copies that could drift.

Kept the "sign in with your phone" link alongside Permit Number lookup rather than replacing it (project owner's own call) -- useful for an owner with more than one business who wants to log in and see everything at once, rather than looking up one business at a time.

**Verified end-to-end against real production data**, not just read: drove the actual `/apply` flow against San Miguel's own real test business (CEZMIL'S, license `7094956`, permit `MS-2026-00001`, real owner phone on file). Confirmed both lookup paths (legacy license number, permit number) resolve to the identical business with `claimed: true` and the correct masked phone; confirmed a genuinely nonexistent number still returns a clean `found: false`. Then completed one real round trip: clicked "Send code," which fired a real SMS via Semaphore to the real phone on file, read the actual generated code back from `otp_codes` directly (no access to that inbox otherwise), submitted it, and confirmed the flow landed straight on that specific business's pre-filled renewal form -- `applyProfile()` correctly pre-filled the real business name, skipping the phone-entry step entirely, exactly as designed.

**Same-day follow-up: two real bugs, reported live by the project owner** ("form was pre-filled, wouldn't show any field for the uploads, says fill in the form required, but I've filled everything already"). Reproduced against the exact same real business/data before touching anything, rather than guessing:

1. **A genuine, pre-existing UX trap, not broken code.** The applicant form's Business Operation section has a real cascading-disclosure chain, already documented in `application-form-logic.ts`'s own comments: "same address?" stays hidden's next question (premises ownership) until answered, which in turn gates employees, which in turn gates barangay clearance/tax incentives. This already existed and works exactly as designed for a fresh "New business" submission, filled top-to-bottom. But a renewal is pre-filled from the business's existing (often legacy-import-sparse) profile, so most of the form already *looks* done -- one cascade-gating field left blank further down, revealing yet another blank required field once answered, is very easy to miss entirely when nothing around it visually signals "answer this to unlock more." The section's own message just said "fill in the required fields above (marked *)" with no indication of *which* -- traced against the project owner's real test data (CEZMIL'S, almost entirely blank besides business name/nature/barangay/gross sales) and confirmed this is exactly what happened. Fixed by having that message list the actual missing fields by name (`missingRequiredFields`, reusing the same `fieldLabel()` helper the submit-error message already uses) instead of leaving the applicant to hunt.
2. **A real bug specific to the new businessId-driven renewal path (7gg, same day).** The form's "Mobile phone" field is read-only, bound to the `phone` client state -- correct for every *other* path, since the applicant always typed it on the earlier "phone" screen. The new renewal-OTP-to-file path skips that screen entirely (the phone is resolved server-side, never typed), so `phone` stayed blank -- and since phone is itself a required field, this silently blocked the Documents section too, stacked on top of bug 1. Fixed by having `verify-otp/route.ts` return the phone it actually checked the OTP against (safe to reveal at this exact point -- verification just proved the applicant received that SMS), and the client now sets `phone` state from that response for every path, not just the ones where it was already known.

Re-verified live against the same real business after both fixes: the "Mobile phone" field now shows the real number immediately post-verification, the missing-fields list correctly dropped "phone" and the operation-address question once answered, and answering that question correctly revealed "Business premises ownership" as the next item on the list -- the cascade mechanism itself confirmed working exactly as intended, just properly surfaced now.

**Same-day follow-up: the lookup field's own labeling didn't communicate what it already accepted.** `lookup-license/route.ts` has searched both `businesses.legacy_license_no` and `applications.reference_number` since this section's own original build, but `ApplyPageClient.tsx`'s field label just said "Permit number," the placeholder only showed a Permit Number-shaped example ("e.g. MS-2026-00001"), and the error/confirmation copy only ever said "Permit Number" -- someone who only had an old License Number (most legacy businesses, since they've never transacted through MuniServe yet) had no visible cue that typing it there would actually work. The project owner asked directly for both to be explicitly supported. No backend change was needed (it already searched both) -- purely a copy fix: the field label is now "Permit No. or License No.," the placeholder shows an example of both, and every user-facing string that used to say just "Permit Number" (the no-match error, the "don't have that number" link, the unclaimed-match confirm screen, the phone-sign-in "no account" error) now says either both explicitly or a generic "that number"/"either number" instead of assuming one specific kind.

Verified end-to-end against real production data, not just the copy change in isolation: typed a real still-unclaimed License Number (`7095975`, GREEN SEASON FOODS PRODUCTS) into the relabeled field on the live dev server against production and confirmed it correctly resolved to that business's confirm screen -- the backend behavior was already correct, this confirms the new labeling didn't accidentally break the lookup itself.

## 7hh. Assessment overrides: a live-updating total, with a deliberate Save step (2026-08-16)

Flagged directly by the project owner: BPLO could type an override amount and a reason on the assessment card, but "Total due online" never reflected it until the whole form was already submitted via "Finalize assessment" -- a real, one-shot action (writes `application_fee_lines`, moves the application to `pending_payment`) with no way to check the resulting number first.

Discussed the exact interaction before building: agreed the total shouldn't update on every keystroke (a half-typed multi-digit override would flash a wrong number), so each override line gets its own **Save** button, enabled only once that line has a typed amount. Clicking Save is what makes that line's override count toward the live total shown on screen. Clearing an override back to blank reverts the total immediately, no Save needed -- a blank override is already unambiguous (`finalizeAssessment`'s own logic already treats `""` as "no override"), so there's nothing to confirm there.

Checked `finalizeAssessment` (`bplo/actions.ts`) first to make sure the live preview and the real submission could never disagree: it already reads `overridden_amount ?? computed_amount` straight from whatever's in the form fields at the moment "Finalize assessment" is clicked, regardless of any client-side "Save" state. So Save is deliberately **only a preview affordance** -- it decides what the live total shows, not what actually gets submitted, and required zero changes to `finalizeAssessment` itself. The actual `<input name="override_...">`/`<input name="overrideReason_...">` elements are unchanged in name/shape; only made controlled client-side to drive the preview.

Extracted `computedLines.map(...)` + the "Total due online" block + the (formerly separate) `AssessmentManualSection` into one new client component, `src/app/dashboard/bplo/assessment-line-items.tsx` (`AssessmentLineItems`) -- overrides and manual entries previously lived in two components each unaware of the other's contribution; now they combine into a single live total. `AssessmentManualSection` itself (assessment-manual-fields.tsx) is deleted as genuinely dead code once nothing renders it anymore -- only its `ManualFieldSpec` type export is still used.

**Verified interactively** (temporary fixture, same technique as this project's other staff-auth-gated UI tests, deleted immediately after -- rendered `AssessmentLineItems` directly with the real fee-line shape/amounts from `MS-2026-00005`): confirmed Save starts disabled with no draft, becomes enabled once a value is typed, and the total only changes after clicking it (not while typing); confirmed clearing an override reverts the total immediately without a Save click; confirmed the Automated-Assessment-off case (manual fields + remaining override-able regulatory lines in the same card) combines both into one correct live total, and that two independent card instances don't leak state into each other.

---

## 7ii. Order of Payment: itemized assessment slip -- PDF, email, and BPLO print/reprint (2026-08-16)

The project owner photographed a real physical San Miguel "Order of Payment" -- what BPLO hands the applicant right after assessment, and what the applicant shows the Treasurer to pay. Asked for three things built together: generate it as a PDF, email it to the applicant, and let BPLO preview/print/reprint it from more than one place. Explicitly asked to plan first before building, per this project's standing pattern -- discussed the design (four clarifying questions, all answered) before any code:

- **Acct Codes**: ship empty/optional, BPLO fills in later; only regulatory fees get an editing UI this pass (LBT/Mayor's Permit have many `fee_rules` rows sharing one conceptual category, unlike regulatory fees' natural 1:1 -- extending Acct Code editing to those is a known follow-up, flagged in Settings' own explanatory text).
- **"Reviewed & Recommended for Approval"**: prints `lgus.treasurer_name`, a plain Settings field with no approval workflow gate behind it -- same shape as `mayor_name` (migration 0033).
- **"Plate No."**: left off the document entirely -- no reuse of `reference_number`, no new counter invented for it.
- **Mode of Payment**: BPLO explicitly picks Annual/Semi-Annual/Quarterly at the moment of finalizing assessment (a required `<select>` on `AssessmentCard`), frozen onto `applications.mode_of_payment` -- not reused from `businesses.business_tax_payment` (the applicant's own stated preference, which can drift after this specific assessment is already finalized, and uses different wording, "Bi-Annually" vs. the real form's "Semi-Annual").

CTC #/Issued On/Issued At, SSS No., and SEC/DTI Date Issued render as blank underlined fields on the generated PDF, not guessed at -- MuniServe captures no CEDULA control number anywhere today, and the applicant form only collects one combined `registrationAuthority` + `registrationNo`, no per-authority date fields, no SSS field at all.

**Schema (migration `0039_order_of_payment.sql`, live in production)**: `fee_rules.acct_code`/`application_fee_lines.acct_code` (the LGU's own municipal revenue-code numbering, e.g. "605-1" -- nullable, denormalized onto `application_fee_lines` at finalize time, same reasoning `fee_category`/`display_label` already are, migration 0026: a reprint months later must show the code that was true *then*, not whatever a since-edited `fee_rules` row says now); `applications.assessment_finalized_by` (mirrors the existing `initial_review_by`/`printed_by`/`released_by` attribution columns -- assessment finalization was the one stage in that pattern missing one, and the Order of Payment's "Assessed by" line needs a name that's still correct on a reprint months later, unlike `audit_log.actor_label`'s point-in-time text snapshot); `applications.mode_of_payment`; `lgus.treasurer_name`. No new RLS policies needed anywhere -- verified directly against `pg_policies` before writing the migration: `fee_rules.acct_code` on regulatory rows is already covered by migration 0027's "bplo can manage regulatory fees" (ALL, not per-column); `lgus.treasurer_name` by 0027's own general "bplo can update their own lgu's settings"; `assessment_finalized_by`/`mode_of_payment` are written via `finalizeAssessment`'s existing service-role client.

**Assessment flow**: `AssessmentCard` (`bplo/page.tsx`) gained a required Mode of Payment `<select>` between the line items and "Finalize assessment." `finalizeAssessment` (`bplo/actions.ts`) validates it's one of the three allowed values before doing anything else, threads `acctCode` through the whole `FinalLine`/`lineRows` pipeline (every fee line source -- computed, manual LBT/Mayor's Permit fallback, the Engineering Building Permit Fee synthetic line -- now carries it through to the `application_fee_lines` insert), and writes `mode_of_payment`/`assessment_finalized_by` onto the same `applications` UPDATE that already sets `status`/`assessment_finalized_at`.

**`src/lib/order-of-payment-pdf.ts`** (`generateOrderOfPaymentPdf`): same pdf-lib/no-Puppeteer convention as every other generated document in this codebase (navy letterhead banner, Letter size, "Php" not "₱" since pdf-lib's standard Helvetica can't encode U+20B1). A reasonable first draft matching the real photo's field set/wording/order, not a pixel-exact recreation -- same framing already established for `print-certificate.ts`'s own from-photo document, since a phone photo can't be reproduced with real graphic-design fidelity either way. Table columns: Acct Code / Particulars / Amount, one row per included `application_fee_lines` row, wrapped for long labels; a Total Amount Due line; the five blank reference fields; two signature blocks ("Assessed by" / "Reviewed & Recommended for Approval").

**Deliberately reads from `application_fee_lines`, never re-running `computeApplicationFees()`** -- same reprint-accuracy reasoning as the denormalized `acct_code`/`display_label` columns themselves: this needs to render correctly months later even if `fee_rules` has since changed.

**`/api/dashboard/order-of-payment`** (new route handler, not a server action -- needs real `Content-Type`/`Content-Disposition` headers): any staff role at the application's own LGU can open it (Treasury needs to see what's being charged too, not just BPLO -- same audience as `application-form-pdf`'s route), `Content-Disposition: inline` so it opens straight in a new tab ready to print, matching `print-permit`'s own convention.

**Wired in two places** (Task 7's own scope: preview/print at assessment stage, reprint later from elsewhere): `payment-queue.tsx`'s `AwaitingPaymentSection` (shared between Treasury's and BPLO's dashboards, so "preview/print right at the assessment stage" is covered automatically the moment an application lands there -- no separate change needed on BPLO's own dashboard) gained an "Open Order of Payment" link next to the existing assessed-total breakdown; the Business Registry's per-business application history gained the same link, shown only once `assessment_finalized_at` is set (an application that hasn't reached assessment yet has nothing to show).

**Email**: `sendEmail()` (`src/lib/resend.ts`) gained an optional `attachments` parameter, using Resend's own REST shape directly (`{filename, content}`, base64). `notifyApplicantEmail()` (`src/lib/notifications.ts`) is new -- a deliberate, flagged one-off exception to this file's own standing comment that applicant notifications are SMS-only (phone is the applicant's real OTP-verified identity, not email) -- built specifically because SMS has no way to carry a PDF attachment. Never the *only* notification for this event: `finalizeAssessment` still sends the existing SMS regardless of whether the owner has an email on file, since phone is guaranteed for every applicant and email isn't. Wired into `finalizeAssessment` right after the existing SMS block -- generates the PDF and emails it only when `business.owner?.email` is present, wrapped in try/catch (best-effort, matching every other notification in this codebase: a PDF-render or Resend failure must never undo an assessment that was already finalized).

**Verified against real production data, not just type-checked**: pulled San Miguel's own real assessed application (`MS-2026-00004`, 1RALTEC LOGISTICS SERVICES -- a renewal with a real BPLO override on Mayor's Permit Fee, 8 included lines, 1 correctly-excluded reference-only CEDULA line, total ₱2,900) and ran `generateOrderOfPaymentPdf()` directly against it, reading the rendered PDF bytes back rather than assuming the code was right -- confirmed the letterhead, itemized table (including the override amount and a test Acct Code), and total all render correctly. Ran it a second time with every optional field deliberately blank (this exact real application predates the feature -- `mode_of_payment`/`assessment_finalized_by`/`treasurer_name` are all still null in production) to confirm the blank-field fallbacks degrade gracefully rather than crashing or printing "null." Also confirmed RLS directly: three rolled-back SQL transactions under a real non-BPLO (`department`-role) staff session at San Miguel successfully read `applications`, `application_fee_lines`, and `lgus` exactly the way the new route does -- confirming the "any staff role at their own LGU" access model actually holds under RLS, not just in the route's own code.
