The project owner asked directly for BPLO's dashboard to stop stacking every pipeline stage on the page at once -- every stage's queue (Initial review, In dept. review, Assessment review, Treasurer approval, For printing, Mayor's signature, For release, Released) used to render simultaneously, always, all the way down the page. Now each of the 8 stat cards is a tab: clicking one shows only that stage's queue below, defaulting to Initial review on load. Confirmed the scope directly before building, since the request could plausibly have meant more: this only applies to BPLO's own dashboard (the one with 8 distinct pipeline stat cards) -- Department's dashboard has exactly one stat card ("Awaiting your review") and one list, no tabs to click, so it's untouched. Within the "In dept. review" tab, every department's decision (including a "request more info") still shows exactly as it always has, inline in that application's own card -- that part of the existing rendering wasn't touched, only gated behind the tab click.

**`src/app/dashboard/bplo/dashboard-tabs.tsx`** (`BploDashboardTabs`), a new client component -- the same "Server Components as children of a Client Component" shape this project's other tabbed view (`audit-trail-table.tsx`'s `AuditTrailExplorer`) already established, not a new pattern. `bplo/page.tsx` still does every bit of its own data fetching and rendering exactly as before (including the async `InitialReviewCard`/`AssessmentCard` components) -- it just builds each stage's section as a named `ReactNode` instead of stacking them all in the returned JSX, then hands all 8 `{statCard, content}` pairs to `BploDashboardTabs`, which only ever decides which one to show. "Needs your review" (previously one combined heading/empty-state for Initial review + Assessment review together, since both render under one stat card's worth of urgency) is split into two standalone tab contents, each with its own heading and empty state, to match the 8 individual stat cards 1:1. `StatCard` itself is untouched -- each tab's stat card is just wrapped in a plain reset `<button>` in the new component, with a `ring-2 ring-brand-teal` applied only to the active one, rather than adding click behavior to the shared component every other dashboard page also uses non-interactively.

**New: a Released tab**, which never had a list anywhere on this page before (only ever a stat count). Purely informational -- nothing left to act on for a released permit -- so no action buttons, just business name/owner/release date and the same permit `pdf_url` download link the applicant's own `/status` page already shows, for handing someone their permit again without needing the applicant's own link. Needed one small addition to the main `applications` query (`released_at`) and a new `permits.pdf_url` lookup keyed by the released application ids, mirroring the existing pattern already used for department rounds/reviews on this same page.

"Returned to applicant" and "Archived" are deliberately left outside this tab mechanism entirely -- they're side states (a rejected/returned application, or a manually closed-out one), not pipeline stages with their own stat card, so they stay exactly as they were: two always-visible `CollapsibleSection`s below the tabbed area.

**Verified interactively, not just by reading the code**: since `/dashboard/bplo` needs a real Google-OAuth staff session that can't be driven in this environment, built a temporary unauthenticated fixture page (deleted immediately after) rendering the real `BploDashboardTabs` component with 8 mock tabs. Confirmed via the actual rendered page text that "Initial review" is the default active tab on load; clicking "In dept. review" and separately "Released" both correctly swapped the displayed content to that tab's own marker text; and confirmed via each button's real `className` (not `getComputedStyle`, which read stale in this specific automated browser environment -- the same class of testing-environment quirk already flagged once before in this project, CLAUDE.md 7nn) that the `ring-2 ring-brand-teal` active-state styling correctly followed whichever tab was actually clicked, not the one clicked before it. Also navigated to the real, unmodified `/dashboard/bplo` route directly (no session) and confirmed it compiles and redirects cleanly to `/login` with zero server errors in the dev log -- proof the real page's full module tree (including the new client component and the split-out tab content) is genuinely wired together correctly, not just the isolated fixture.

## 7ss. Performance Stats -> Stats & Reports, with a new Reports tab (2026-08-17)

The project owner asked to rename "Performance Stats" to "Stats & Reports," split into two sub-tabs (Performance -- unchanged, exactly what already existed; Reports -- new), and asked for revenue numbers: how much has been collected for Barangay Clearance, Engineering, CEDULA, and "Actual Permit" (LBT + Mayor's Permit Fee + every other regulatory fee), each downloadable. Discussed one real design fork before building, since it materially changes the numbers: collected (only applications Treasury has actually recorded a payment for) vs. assessed/billed (everything charged, paid or not). The project owner confirmed collected -- this is a revenue report, not a billing report.

**A real schema nuance surfaced and confirmed against production before building anything**: "Engineering" isn't its own `fee_category` -- Engineering's Building Permit Fee (CLAUDE.md 7aa) is written as an ordinary `regulatory`-category line (`AssessmentCard`'s `engineeringLine`), just with `fee_rule_id: null` since it's a typed-in figure with no corresponding `fee_rules` row. That's the one thing that reliably separates it from every other regulatory fee (CNC, Health Permit Fee, Inspection Fee, Plate Fee, Sanitary Fee, ...) -- confirmed directly against real production data (`fee_rule_id is null` cleanly isolated exactly the 3 real "Building Permit Fee" lines, nothing else) before relying on it in the query.

**A real, pre-existing data gap found while building this, fixed at the source**: 6 `application_fee_lines` rows (from San Miguel's very first two pilot applications, MS-2026-00002/00003) had `fee_category = null` -- they predate migration 0026's denormalization of `fee_category`/`display_label` onto this table at write time. Two were reference_only CEDULA (correctly excluded already via `included_in_total`), but the other four were ₱21,000 of real LBT/Mayor's Permit revenue that would have silently fallen through any category-based report. Fixed with migration `0048_backfill_application_fee_lines_category.sql` (live in production) -- backfills both columns from each row's own `fee_rule_id -> fee_rules.fee_category/name`, the same join every one of these rows already has, rather than working around it at query time. Benefits any future query against this table, not just this one report.

**`src/lib/revenue-report.ts`** (`computeRevenueReport`), matching `performance-stats.ts`'s own "computation lives separate from rendering" split: takes a plain `supabase` client (no cookie-session dependency, callable directly, no re-implementation needed to verify). Finds every application with a `payments` row in the date range at this LGU (an application with more than one payment row is counted once, keyed off its earliest `received_at` in range), then pulls every `included_in_total = true` fee line for those applications and classifies each into one of four buckets: `barangay_clearance`, `engineering` (regulatory + null `fee_rule_id`), `cedula`, and `actual_permit` (LBT + Mayor's Permit Fee + every other regulatory fee, net of the essential-commodity discount, which is really just a reduction of the LBT line). "Actual Permit" also gets its own 3-way sub-breakdown (LBT / Mayor's Permit Fee / other regulatory) so BPLO can see what's inside the combined number without opening the CSV.

**`src/app/dashboard/stats/sub-nav.tsx`** (`StatsSubNav`): same route-per-view shape as `BusinessesSubNav` (Directory vs. Permit History) -- Performance and Reports are two different questions with their own server-side data fetching, not one page client-toggling between two datasets it both fetched up front. `/dashboard/stats` (Performance, unchanged data/logic, just gained the sub-nav) and new `/dashboard/stats/reports` (Reports). The shared nav pill (`ui.tsx`'s `DashboardTopBar`) is relabeled "Stats & Reports" -- same `statsHref` gating (BPLO + Mayor) as before, no change to who can see it.

**CSV export**: same client-side generation this app already established (`audit-trail-table.tsx`'s own `exportCSV` -- a `Blob` + object URL, no server route), in a new `export-buttons.tsx`. Both what the project owner explicitly asked for ("each of them must be downloadable" -- a per-category "Download CSV" next to each of the 4 buckets) and a combined "Download all as CSV" at the top, since a single LGU-wide file is also genuinely useful and costs nothing extra to add.

**Two things added beyond exactly what was asked, flagged here rather than silently assumed wanted**: a CEDULA online/counter-paid caveat note (only shown when `lgu.cedulaIncludedOnline` is false) -- without it, an LGU with CEDULA set to counter-paid would see a near-zero CEDULA figure and could easily read that as MuniServe losing track of real revenue, when it's actually just being collected outside the system entirely (CLAUDE.md 7ff). And the "Actual Permit" sub-breakdown itself -- the project owner asked for one combined number; showing what's inside it doesn't change that number, just adds transparency for anyone auditing it later.

**Verified against real production data, not just logic**: cross-checked the exact bucket classification with a hand-written SQL query first (`case when fee_category = ... then ... end`, grouped and summed independently of any application code), then called the real, unmodified `computeRevenueReport()` directly against San Miguel's production data (temporary fixture, deleted immediately after -- the function needed no re-implementation, since it takes a plain client and has no session dependency) and confirmed the output matched the independent SQL exactly: ₱350 Barangay Clearance (1 app), ₱1,420 Engineering (3 apps, all labeled "Building Permit Fee"), ₱0 CEDULA (this LGU's real setting), ₱31,840 Actual Permit (5 apps, breaking down to ₱25,040 LBT / ₱2,000 Mayor's Permit / ₱4,800 other regulatory), ₱33,610 grand total across 6 paid applications. Also confirmed date-range filtering genuinely excludes out-of-range payments (a range with zero real payments returned all zeros; narrowing to a single real day returned a smaller, internally-consistent total, not the full range's numbers). Both `/dashboard/stats` and the new `/dashboard/stats/reports` were confirmed to compile and redirect cleanly with zero server errors when visited unauthenticated.

**Same-day follow-up: the per-category detail list was a very long, repetitive flat list, not a report.** The project owner shared a screenshot of the first version -- "Actual Permit" listed every one of an application's fee lines as its own row, so a business with 8 fee lines showed its own name 8 times in a row, one line at a time. Real feedback, and a real design gap: no summary, no way to scan by business.

Redesigned into a real three-level drill-down, matching exactly what the project owner described: each category (Barangay Clearance/Engineering/CEDULA/Actual Permit) is now its own collapsed `<details>` showing just a total (application count + amount); expanding it reveals one row per application/business with that business's own subtotal in this category, still collapsed; expanding a business row reveals its individual fee lines only then. Grouping happens client-side in `reports/page.tsx` (`groupLinesByApplication`) from the exact same flat `lines` array `computeRevenueReport()` already returns -- no change to that function, this was purely a rendering concern. Reuses the same native `<details>`/`<summary>` + `.chev` rotation mechanism this codebase already established (`CollapsibleSection`, `globals.css`'s `details[open] > summary .chev` rule, which works for any nested details/summary pair without extra CSS), just nested one level deeper -- no client JS needed for the expand/collapse itself, matching this project's own "plain server action forms/native disclosure widgets over client state where possible" convention. The per-category "Download CSV" button still exports every line in that category regardless of which rows are expanded.

**Verified against real production data, not just logic**: a temporary fixture (deleted immediately after) called the real, unmodified `computeRevenueReport()` against San Miguel's production data and rendered the exact same nested structure. Confirmed the grouping is byte-correct against the same real applications checked earlier in this section -- "Benj Computer Shop" now renders as two separate, correctly-labeled collapsed rows (MS-2026-00002 and MS-2026-00005, its two different applications) instead of repeating per fee line, and every business's subtotal sums correctly to its own fee lines (verified all 5 Actual Permit subtotals individually, e.g. 1RALTEC LOGISTICS SERVICES's 8 lines summing to exactly ₱2,540) and to the category total (₱31,840). Drove real clicks (not scripted attribute toggles) at both the category level and a nested business level and confirmed each genuinely opened independently of the other rows around it, which stayed collapsed.

## 7tt. Settings redesign: grouped, not a flat 12-item list (2026-08-18)

The project owner shared a screenshot and asked directly for Settings to stop looking "messy, scattered, unorganized" -- a fair read: it had grown to 12 sections (Add/Remove Staff plus 11 `CollapsibleSection`s) added one at a time across many earlier passes, each just a chevron + bold title + gray description in a flat vertical list with no visual grouping at all, everyone's own 2.25rem bottom margin the only thing separating one setting from the next.

**Regrouped into 6 labeled categories, each its own bordered card with an icon header**, rather than redesigning any individual section's own content (every form, toggle, and upload control inside each `CollapsibleSection` is untouched):
- **Staff & Access** -- Add/Remove Staff
- **Fee Rates** -- Business Tax & Mayor's Permit Fee Setup, Regulatory Fee Flat Amounts
- **Barangays** -- Barangays, Barangay Clearance (kept adjacent and in this exact order deliberately -- CLAUDE.md 7nn's own same-day follow-up already established this ordering on purpose, since per-barangay clearance rates depend on the barangay list existing first; a naive alphabetical or fee-only regrouping would have broken that)
- **Assessment Rules** -- Automated Assessment, Building Permit Fee (Engineering), CEDULA
- **Documents & Alerts** -- Permit Certificate Details, Order of Payment Details, SMS Notifications
- **Public Application Form** -- Your public application form

**New `SettingsGroup` (`dashboard/ui.tsx`)** -- an icon chip + title + one-line description, wrapping a bordered `rounded-3xl` card that its `CollapsibleSection` children render inside. Deliberately does **not** modify `CollapsibleSection` itself, which stays exactly as it already was for its other caller (`bplo/page.tsx`'s Returned/Archived sections) -- `SettingsGroup` only adds the surrounding container and, via a scoped Tailwind child-selector (`[&>details]:mb-0`), zeroes out each nested `CollapsibleSection`'s own bottom margin so a `divide-y` border between items reads as one connected list inside the card instead of each item still carrying its own independent gap on top of the new grouping. A shared visual language for the icon chips (`bg-surface-3`, brand-navy/teal text) rather than the semantic good/warn/bad status tones already used elsewhere on this page -- these are just category labels, not status indicators, and coloring them like a warning/success state would send the wrong signal.

One new hand-authored icon (`BellIcon`, matching this codebase's existing stroke-based SVG convention exactly) for Documents & Alerts; every other group reuses an icon that already existed (`UserIcon`, `PinIcon`, `SettingsIcon`, `FileIcon`) or a literal "₱" glyph for Fee Rates, rather than inventing a peso-coin icon for a symbol that's already unambiguous as plain text. Also added a real page-level header ("Settings" + a one-line description) -- the page previously had no H1 or intro text at all, going straight into the first section, which was itself part of why it read as an undifferentiated list rather than a page with a clear top.

**Verified interactively, not just visually assumed**: since `/dashboard/settings` needs a real staff session that can't be driven here, built a temporary fixture page (deleted immediately after) mirroring the real page's exact 6-group structure with representative mock content per section. Confirmed via the accessibility tree that all 6 groups and every nested section render in the right order with their content intact; confirmed via computed styles that the child-margin override actually took effect (`0px`, not the original `36px`); drove a real click (not a scripted attribute toggle) on one collapsed item and confirmed it genuinely expanded while its sibling stayed collapsed; and checked computed colors in dark mode (card background, border, and icon-chip text all resolved to distinct, readable values -- no invisible-border or black-on-black failure). Also confirmed the real, unmodified `/dashboard/settings` and `/dashboard/bplo` (the other `CollapsibleSection` caller) both still compile and render with zero server errors.

## 7uu. Self-service legacy business roster import (2026-08-19)

The project owner flagged a real, foundational onboarding gap ahead of a planned full-system audit: San Miguel's own 1,177-row legacy business roster (`supabase/seed/legacy_business_import.sql`) was seeded via a one-time, developer-run SQL script -- there was no way for a NEW LGU to bring in their own existing business list (an Excel/CSV export from whatever they used before MuniServe) without a developer writing that script by hand each time. Discussed the design before building, per this project's standing pattern, since two real decisions materially change the implementation:

- **Basis is COLLECTED-when-possible, not always-unclaimed.** The original San Miguel import had no phone/email data at all, so every row imported `is_legacy_unclaimed = true`. A new LGU's own export might actually have phone numbers on file -- confirmed with the project owner that when a row DOES include a phone, MuniServe should create a real owner and mark that business claimed immediately, so that applicant can renew online right away with no separate claim step. A row with no phone still imports unclaimed, exactly like before.
- **Scoped to the Business Registry only**, not `permit_history` (the separate historical-transactions log behind the Permit History reporting page, CLAUDE.md 7f) -- confirmed this import should feed what actually enables renewals/claims, not a second reporting dataset, which stays out of scope for now.

**`src/lib/business-import.ts`**: a pure parse/validate module, matching `fee-rule-import.ts`'s own established shape exactly (same preview-before-publish pattern, same CSV parser reused rather than duplicated -- `parseCsv`/`toCsv` are imported from that file, not reimplemented). Columns match the original San Miguel import's real shape (`business_name`, `legacy_license_no`, `barangay`, `address`, `nature_of_business`, `lbt_category`, `organization_type`, `tin`, `legacy_owner_name`, `gross_sales`) plus the two new ones the project owner asked for (`phone`, `email`). A missing `legacy_license_no` is a warning, not an error -- that business just can't be found by number lookup, but can still be claimed by phone at the counter or given a number later. An invalid `phone` (fails `normalizePhone()`) degrades the row to unclaimed rather than rejecting it outright.

**`src/app/dashboard/settings/business-import-actions.ts`**: `previewBusinessImport` (parse-only) and `publishBusinessImport` (re-parses server-side, never trusts the client's own preview state -- same reasoning as every other CSV import in this project). Two real-world details resolved before writing the actual insert logic, not guessed at:
- `businesses.legacy_license_no` is **globally unique**, not per-LGU (confirmed by reading the schema directly) -- a row whose license number already exists anywhere is silently skipped, not treated as a hard error blocking the whole file. This also makes the feature insert-only and safely repeatable: uploading a file that includes previously-imported rows is a no-op for those specific rows, new rows still get added. Deliberately does **not** delete-and-replace on re-run the way the original one-time SQL script did (`"Safe to re-run: deletes San Miguel's legacy-unclaimed businesses first"`) -- that made sense for a single developer-run setup step, not for a repeatable self-service feature where deleting existing rows on every re-upload would be a real, avoidable risk.
- `lbt_category` is only checked against a real value once the LGU is known (`getLbtCategoryOptions`, already existing) -- an unrecognized code imports the business anyway with the category left blank and a warning, matching the exact same "missing LBT category" gap this project already has a real, working fallback for (CLAUDE.md 7p -- BPLO sets it later from the Business Registry) rather than blocking the whole row over one bad column.

**Batched, not row-by-row** -- a real LGU's roster can be 1,000+ rows (San Miguel's own original import was 1,177), so unlike the fee-rule CSV import's own per-row insert loop (fine for ~110 rows), this resolves owners and duplicates in bulk lookups first, then does one single multi-row `businesses` insert. Two businesses in the same file sharing one phone number (a real case -- rule #3, one owner can have multiple businesses) correctly resolve to the SAME owner, created once, not twice -- verified directly, not assumed. Owner creation reuses the exact same client-generated-UUID-insert-without-`.select()` pattern already established in `businesses/actions.ts`'s `claimLegacyBusiness`/`startWalkInApplication` (CLAUDE.md 7ee's own RLS-RETURNING gotcha). An existing owner found by phone match is never overwritten (name/email) by the import -- only a brand-new owner gets the imported name/email, so a stale export file can't clobber a real owner's already-live data.

**Migration `0049_bplo_insert_businesses.sql`** (live in production): nothing has ever inserted into `businesses` through a real staff RLS session before this -- every existing row came from the one-time seed script or the applicant-facing submit route, both service-role. New BPLO-scoped INSERT policy, same shape as the existing "bplo can update businesses at their own lgu" policy.

**`src/app/api/dashboard/business-import-template/route.ts`**: always the example template, not a round-trip of an LGU's existing businesses the way the fee-rule template is -- a real roster could be 1,000+ rows, nothing useful to re-download and re-edit; this is a one-time-ish onboarding action, not a yearly-revised rate table.

**UI**: new `BusinessImportCard` (`business-import.tsx`), same multi-step preview-then-publish shape as `FeeRuleImportCard`, plus a "Tax year" field (applies to every row's `gross_sales_history` entry, matching the original single-year-snapshot convention). Surfaced as a new "Data Import" `SettingsGroup`, positioned right after Staff & Access -- both are "get this LGU set up" tasks.

**Verified against real production data, not just logic**: confirmed via a rolled-back SQL transaction that a real BPLO session at San Miguel can insert a `businesses` row (the new policy), and a `department`-role session at the same LGU cannot. Confirmed the actual write logic (owner resolution, duplicate skipping, category validation) with a temporary fixture route (deleted immediately after) replicating `publishBusinessImport`'s exact steps via service-role against a synthetic 5-row file at San Miguel's real LGU id, including one row that collided with a pre-seeded "already imported" license number: the duplicate was correctly skipped (4 imported, 1 skipped), two businesses sharing one phone number resolved to the exact same real owner id (not two), a business with no phone imported correctly unclaimed, and a business with an unrecognized `lbt_category` imported successfully with the category nulled and a warning surfaced -- all re-read back from the database afterward to confirm the persisted state, not just trusted from the write call's own response. All synthetic businesses and the shared owner deleted afterward -- confirmed zero leftover data in production.

**Same-day follow-up: "how does an LGU add an LBT category? I don't see a section for it."** A fair question -- there genuinely isn't a separate "add a category" feature, because a category and its rates are the same thing in this schema (rule #1: no fee/rate is ever hardcoded, always a real row backed by the actual ordinance). Categories are created as a side effect of the existing "Business Tax & Mayor's Permit Fee Setup" CSV upload (CLAUDE.md 7s) -- each unique `category_code` row in the LBT half of that upload becomes one selectable category. That's real and correct, but the labeling never said so, and the two places someone would actually notice a category is missing (BPLO's Initial Review card, the Business Registry's own LBT category editor) had no pointer back to where to fix it.

Fixed at every point of actual confusion, not just the Settings page itself: the "Business Tax & Mayor's Permit Fee Setup" section's own description now says plainly that this is where categories come from; `FeeRuleImportCard`'s description gets an LBT-specific addition explaining that a new `category_code` row is a new category, no separate step; both LBT-category `<select>` dropdowns (`bplo/page.tsx`'s `InitialReviewCard`, `businesses/page.tsx`'s `RegistryRow`) gained a small "Don't see it? Add it in Settings" link, right where someone would actually be standing when they hit this; and the Import Businesses warning for an unrecognized `lbt_category` now names the exact path to add it, not just where to fix the one business. Pure copy/labeling -- no logic, schema, or data changes, so verified with a full `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` pass plus confirming all three touched routes (`/dashboard/bplo`, `/dashboard/businesses`, `/dashboard/settings`) still render with zero server errors.

**Same-day follow-up: three real gaps in the import template, found by the project owner actually reading it -- one of them a genuine bug, not just a missing column.** Reviewing the template, they noticed it had no owner gender, no payment frequency (Annual/Bi-Annual/Quarterly), and no new-vs-renewal indicator.

- **`business_tax_payment`** is a real, already-existing column (`businesses.business_tax_payment`, the same field the applicant form itself writes) that this import simply never included -- a straightforward omission, fixed by adding the column and validating against the exact same `BUSINESS_TAX_PAYMENT_OPTIONS` the applicant form already uses (`san-miguel-form-options.ts`), not a new invented value set.
- **A real bug surfaced while fixing this**: `organization_type` was being validated against `"individual"`/`"juridical"` -- that pairing is `fee-engine.ts`'s own *internal* CEDULA classification, derived FROM `organization_type` (`organizationType.toLowerCase() === "sole proprietorship"`), not what the column actually stores. Every business imported with a real organization type (`"Sole Proprietorship"`, `"Partnership"`, etc.) would have been silently rejected and imported blank. Fixed to validate against the real `ORGANIZATION_TYPE_OPTIONS` set.
- **Owner gender** maps to `owners.gender`, not a `businesses` column -- added as `owner_gender`, only ever applied when a business is claimed immediately (a row with no phone has no owner to attach it to, and now says so explicitly as a warning rather than silently dropping the value with no explanation).
- **New-vs-renewal** discussed directly before building, since it doesn't have a natural home: a *business* isn't new or renewal, only a specific *application* is, and a business can have both across different years -- storing it as a hard workflow fact would misrepresent what it actually means. Project owner's own call: keep it purely informational. New `businesses.legacy_application_type` (migration `0050`, live in production), display-only, shown in the Business Registry next to the existing License No. note -- no workflow, fee, or eligibility logic reads it.

**Verified against real production data**: a temporary fixture (deleted immediately after) ran the real `parseBusinessImportCsv()` and the same write logic `publishBusinessImport()` now uses against two synthetic rows at San Miguel's real LGU id -- one with fully valid values, one with deliberately invalid `organization_type`/`owner_gender`/`business_tax_payment`. Confirmed the valid row persisted `organization_type: "Sole Proprietorship"` (proving the bug fix), `business_tax_payment: "Quarterly"`, `legacy_application_type: "Renewal"`, and a real new owner with `gender: "Female"` correctly attached; confirmed the invalid row degraded every bad field to `null` with the expected warning text, correctly flagged that a given `owner_gender` was ignored since that row had no phone/owner to attach it to, and still imported `legacy_application_type: "New"` untouched (purely informational, no validation needed). All synthetic rows deleted afterward -- confirmed zero leftover data in production.

## 7vv. Permit No. Format: configurable reference-number shape per LGU (2026-08-19)

The project owner's own words: "our 3rd task is about the reference number or the Permit No.... it has a format. But we need to allow LGU to come up with their own formats... three fields in a row because that usually is the format of the LGU, for example in San Miguel Bulacan, their format is SMB - 2026 - 000056." Before this, every piece of `MS-2026-00001`'s shape -- the "MS" prefix, the 4-digit year, the 5-digit zero-padded counter -- was hardcoded directly in `generate_application_reference()`, a live Postgres function with no LGU-level configurability at all.

**Investigated the real mechanism before proposing anything**, per this project's own standing pattern: read the function's actual live definition (`lgus.short_code` as prefix, a hyphen, `p_year` as passed in by the caller, another hyphen, `lpad(counter, 5, '0')`), confirmed both real call sites (`submit-application/route.ts`, `businesses/actions.ts`'s `startWalkInApplication`) compute `p_year` the same way (`new Date().getFullYear()`, live, never stored), and confirmed `application_reference_counters (lgu_id, year, last_number)` genuinely resets per real calendar year per LGU -- the one behavior explicitly required to survive any redesign. Also chased down and closed a real research dead end: an earlier query appeared to show a second, unexplained `lgus.reference_number` column -- turned out to be a bug in that query's own missing parentheses around an `OR`, not a real column; a clean `information_schema.columns` query confirmed no such column exists.

**One genuine design fork, resolved with the project owner before writing anything**: the project owner described all three fields identically ("if they put 3 or 4 or 2 or 1 letters, it should be accepted... the same goes to the second field"), which reads ambiguously for field 2 specifically -- does the year field stay live-computed (LGU only picks a display width), or become fully static free text the LGU types once and has to remember to update every January? Asked directly rather than guessed, since this is a real behavior fork with a real failure mode (a forgotten January edit silently misdating every permit issued that month) -- confirmed: field 2 stays live-computed, LGU only chooses 2-digit or 4-digit display.

**Migration `0051_permit_number_format.sql`** (live in production, applied only after explicit approval -- the session's own auto-mode classifier flagged this as a direct schema/function write, same as migration 0028's own precedent): two new columns on `lgus`, `reference_year_digits` (check constraint `in (2, 4)`) and `reference_counter_digits` (check constraint `between 3 and 8`), both defaulted to match the current hardcoded behavior exactly (4, 5) so every existing LGU's number format is byte-identical the moment this shipped -- confirmed directly, not assumed (see verification below). Field 1 (prefix) needed no new column at all -- it's the existing `lgus.short_code`, now exposed as BPLO-editable rather than a fixed onboarding-time value. `generate_application_reference()` rewritten to read all three from the LGU's own row instead of hardcoding them; the counter itself still keys off the real, full `p_year` integer regardless of display width, so the per-calendar-year reset guarantee is untouched. No new RLS policy needed -- migration 0027's existing general "BPLO can update their own LGU's settings" policy already covers writing all three columns (`short_code` included), same "RLS bounds rows, not columns" convention this schema uses throughout.

**Settings UI**: a new "Permit No. Format" section (`permit-number-format.tsx`), first item in the "Documents & Alerts" group -- the permit number appears on both the certificate and the Order of Payment slip already in that group. Three fields in a row, exactly as described: a free-text prefix (1-8 letters/numbers, auto-uppercased), a year-width `<select>` (2 or 4 digits), and a counter-width `<select>` (3-8 digits). The one piece of real client interactivity in this otherwise plain-server-action-form page is a live preview string ("Preview of the next permit number: SMB-26-000001") that updates as BPLO types/selects, plus disabling Save on an invalid prefix before it ever reaches the server -- the actual authoritative validation still lives in `updatePermitNumberFormat` (`settings/actions.ts`), which never trusts the client's own preview state, same reasoning as every other Settings form in this app.

**Verified against real production data, not just logic**: three sequential SQL calls, each wrapped in its own `begin...rollback` transaction so nothing touched San Miguel's real live sequence. First confirmed the migration's defaults reproduce San Miguel's exact current format unchanged (`generate_application_reference()` under the new function body returned `MS-2026-00009`, the correct next number given the real committed counter of 8). Second, inside the same rolled-back transaction, reconfigured to 2-digit year / 4-digit counter and confirmed the format actually changed to `MS-26-0010`, then changed the prefix too and got `SMB-26-0011` -- and confirmed the underlying counter kept incrementing off the same real 2026 row throughout (the reset-per-real-year guarantee holds regardless of display width). Third, re-queried the real committed table afterward and confirmed both the counter (still 8) and the LGU's config (still `MS`/4/5) were completely untouched -- proving the rollbacks genuinely discarded every test mutation rather than leaking into production. The client component's live preview and disabled-Save-on-invalid-input behavior were verified interactively via a temporary unauthenticated fixture route (deleted immediately after) -- confirmed the preview string updates correctly as all three fields change together, Save disables on a prefix containing a space/symbol, and re-enables once corrected. `tsc --noEmit` and a full `next build` both ran clean, and the real `/dashboard/settings` route was confirmed to still compile and redirect cleanly to `/login` with zero server errors.

## 7ww. Business Tax installment payments: Bi-Annually/Quarterly, with reminders (2026-08-19)

The project owner's own request: a New application should always pay the full annual Business Tax (Local Business Tax); only a Renewal should get to choose Annual/Bi-Annually/Quarterly, and that choice should actually change what's charged and drive a reminder for the rest. Confirmed `businessTaxPayment` was already a real field on the applicant form (collected on every submission since section 7d, written to `businesses.business_tax_payment`) but never once read by `fee-engine.ts` -- every application was charged the full annual LBT regardless of what was picked, and BPLO's own separate "Mode of Payment" dropdown on the assessment card (Order of Payment, section 7ii) had never been tied to the applicant's choice at all.

**One real inconsistency in the request, resolved with the project owner before building**: dividing the annual amount by three for Quarterly doesn't reconcile with the three reminder dates given (Apr 5 / Jul 5 / Oct 5) -- if the amount charged now is one of three equal shares, only two shares would be left to remind about. Confirmed the intended shape is the standard LGC quarterly schedule instead: **divide by four** (Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct), the first quarter charged now, the three reminders covering the three remaining quarters -- this is what actually lines up with the three dates given. Bi-Annually divides by two (Jan/Jul), unambiguous. **Second confirmed decision**: this ships reminder-only -- MuniServe texts/emails a reminder on each configured date, but does not track a balance or let the applicant pay the remaining installment online. That's a materially bigger feature (a real payment-tracking schema, a new applicant-facing "pay now" step) that wasn't asked for; the project owner's own explicit choice, not assumed.

**Reminder dates are LGU-configurable, not hardcoded** -- the project owner's own words, unprompted: "those dates I gave you is set by San Miguel, I'm not sure if other LGUs are doing the same dates... perhaps we should just add another field they can set the date instead of us hardcoding it?" Agreed and built exactly that.

**Migration `0052_business_tax_installments.sql`** (live in production): `lgus.lbt_biannual_reminder_dates`/`lgus.lbt_quarterly_reminder_dates`, both `text[]` of `'MM-DD'` strings (no year -- the same dates apply every year), null/empty meaning "not configured, no reminders scheduled." San Miguel's real dates (`{'07-05'}` / `{'04-05','07-05','10-05'}`) are seeded directly, since they're a known fact for the pilot LGU, not a guess -- every other LGU (including the real second client, Cavite) starts blank. New `business_tax_reminders` table (`application_id`, `lgu_id`, `reminder_date`, `amount`, `sent_at`) -- one row per scheduled reminder, `amount` snapshotted at creation (same "denormalize at the moment of truth" convention as `application_fee_lines.display_label`/`acct_code`, migration 0026) so a later fee-rule edit can't retroactively change what a reminder claims is due. RLS enabled with deliberately **no policy at all** -- confirmed with the project owner before building: this table is only ever touched by `finalizeAssessment` (insert, service-role) and the new cron (select/update, service-role), never by a staff or anon session, matching `otp_codes`/`application_reference_counters`'s own established posture (CLAUDE.md 7q's follow-up).

**`fee-engine.ts`**: `FeeComputationInput.business.businessTaxPayment` is new. The LBT block now keeps two separate amounts -- `lbtAmount` (the full annual figure, unchanged in every other respect) and `lbtChargedAmount` (divided by `LBT_INSTALLMENT_DIVISOR` -- 2 for Bi-Annually, 4 for Quarterly, 1 otherwise). This split matters: Mayor's Permit Fee's own `preceding_year_lbt_paid` tiering (the ₱150/₱250/₱350 standard-fallback bracket, section 7g) still reads `lbtAmount` -- the real full-year figure -- so choosing Quarterly doesn't silently shrink Mayor's Permit Fee's own tier just because this year's *charged* LBT installment happens to be smaller. Only the pushed LBT line and the essential-commodity discount (which is a rate reduction on whatever's actually being charged this round) use `lbtChargedAmount`. A note is attached to a divided line (e.g. *"Quarterly installment (1 of 4) — the full annual Local Business Tax is ₱4,400."*) so BPLO always sees the full-year figure even when only a slice is charged. Verified directly against a real production business (Benj Computer Shop, MS-2026-00005's own real data -- gross sales ₱300,000, LBT Schedule "wholesaler"): Annual → LBT ₱4,400/total ₱6,700; Bi-Annually → LBT ₱2,200/total ₱4,500; Quarterly → LBT ₱1,100/total ₱3,400; Mayor's Permit Fee stayed exactly ₱350 in all three cases, confirming the full-annual/charged-amount split works as designed.

**Mode of Payment is no longer a separate BPLO pick.** `finalizeAssessment` (`bplo/actions.ts`) now derives it directly from `business.business_tax_payment` (defaulting to `"Annual"` for a pre-feature application or a walk-in filing that never had a chance to set this -- `startWalkInApplication` doesn't re-collect the ~40-field profile, unchanged design from section 7e), instead of reading a `modeOfPayment` form field BPLO picked independently. `AssessmentCard` (`bplo/page.tsx`) shows it as a read-only pill instead of a `<select>`, with the exact same derivation/fallback so the live preview and the actual charge can never disagree. The old `MODE_OF_PAYMENT_VALUES` set (`"Semi-Annual"`) is gone -- the canonical stored value is now `"Bi-Annually"` everywhere (matching `business_tax_payment` and `permit_history`'s own existing vocabulary), with a **display-only** relabel to `"Semi-Annual"` (`order-of-payment-pdf.ts`'s `displayModeOfPayment()`) at the one place that actually needs the real physical Order of Payment's government-document wording -- not a second stored vocabulary.

**Reminder scheduling** happens inside `finalizeAssessment`, right after the fee-lines insert succeeds: for a Bi-Annually/Quarterly application, reads the LGU's configured dates, filters to only dates still ahead of the finalize instant (an application finalized in August has no business "reminding" about an April date already past -- confirmed as a real, correct behavior via production data below), snapshots the LBT line's final charged amount (override included), and inserts one `business_tax_reminders` row per remaining date. Best-effort, matching every other notification in this function -- a scheduling failure must never undo an assessment that was already finalized and charged.

**New daily cron**, `/api/cron/business-tax-installment-reminders` (`vercel.json`, same `CRON_SECRET` auth as `department-reminders`/`cleanup-orphaned-uploads`): scans `business_tax_reminders` for anything due (`reminder_date <= today`, `sent_at is null`), texts (and emails, if on file) the owner with the amount and reference number, marks it sent. Per-row `try`/`catch`, matching `cleanup-orphaned-uploads`' own resilience shape -- one bad row must never block every other applicant's reminder for the day.

**Applicant form**: a New application shows Business Tax Payment as a locked, read-only "Annual" (`ApplyPageClient.tsx`'s `renderField`, special-cased the same way CEDULA's document requirement was special-cased outside the conditional-rule engine, section 7ff) with an explanatory note, instead of the real 3-way select -- Renewal is unaffected. `buildVisibleValues()` overrides the field to `"Annual"` for New so the existing required-field/`readyForDocuments` check doesn't wait on a value the applicant is never shown a control for. **Never trusted client-side alone**: `submit-application/route.ts` forces `businessTaxPayment` to `"Annual"` server-side whenever `applicationType === "new"`, regardless of what the request body claims -- the UI lock is a convenience, not the actual guarantee.

**Verified end-to-end against real production data, not just logic**: the fee-engine division (above) confirmed against Benj Computer Shop's real figures via a temporary fixture route calling the real, unmodified `computeApplicationFees()` directly (deleted immediately after). Reminder scheduling confirmed two ways via a second temporary fixture (deleted immediately after): a pure date-filtering check against synthetic mixed past/future dates (correctly kept only the two future ones), and a full synthetic-application round-trip using San Miguel's own real configured dates -- correctly produced **zero** Bi-Annual reminders (`07-05` had already passed this year) and **exactly one** Quarterly reminder (`10-05`, the only remaining future date), inserted the real row, read it back, then deleted everything with zero leftover data confirmed afterward. RLS confirmed via a rolled-back transaction: a real BPLO session at San Miguel can write both new `lgus` columns (migration 0027's existing general policy already covers them, no new policy needed), with production's real configured dates confirmed untouched afterward. The Settings card was confirmed to pre-fill correctly from real LGU data via a temporary fixture (deleted immediately after). The real `/apply`, `/dashboard/bplo`, and `/dashboard/settings` routes were confirmed to compile and render/redirect cleanly with zero server errors -- including clicking through to the New-business phone-entry screen on the real page, confirming the `path`-based rendering changes don't break the wizard's state machine. The deeper OTP-gated form screen (where the locked Business Tax Payment field actually renders) couldn't be driven without spending a real SMS credit against an uncontrolled number, so that specific screen was verified by code review and the server-side enforcement above rather than a live click-through -- flagged here rather than silently assumed, though the financially-significant part (what actually gets charged) is fully covered either way since the server never trusts the client's value for this field regardless of what the UI shows.
