## 7xx. Accepted Payment Methods (2026-08-19)

The project owner's own request: let BPLO configure which payment channels their LGU actually accepts -- Cash (Over the Counter), GCash (with a number and account name), Bank Transfer (bank name, account number, account name), and an Online Portal (a URL, typically a Landbank LinkBiz link) -- and have whichever ones are turned on show up in the payment-due notification, instead of today's generic "Pay at the Treasurer's Office to continue." Two real forks confirmed with the project owner before building: **multiple channels can be active at once** (an LGU can genuinely accept cash AND GCash AND bank transfer simultaneously, not just one), and **GCash/Bank Transfer/Online each get a proof-of-payment upload** since none of them go through MuniServe directly -- Treasury has no other way to know a GCash/bank transfer/online payment actually happened.

**A real discovery before building anything**: the proof-of-payment upload mechanism the project owner was asking for turned out to already exist. `UploadCard`/`AdditionalDocumentUpload` (`status/[reference]/page.tsx`) has rendered a generic "have a document to add?" prompt at `pending_payment` since the `info_requests` pass (section 7ll) -- originally built for exactly this shape of problem, the BFP payment-proof screenshot case (section 7c). No new upload flow was needed; what was actually missing was (a) telling the applicant *which* channels are available and their specific details, (b) making the upload prompt read as "upload your payment proof" instead of the generic wording when a non-cash channel is enabled, and (c) giving staff anywhere to actually *see* what got uploaded before recording payment -- `AwaitingPaymentSection` (`payment-queue.tsx`, shared by Treasury and BPLO) had never shown an application's documents at all.

**Migration `0053_lgu_payment_methods.sql`** (live in production): ten new flat columns on `lgus` -- `accepts_cash_counter` (default `true`, matching every existing LGU's real unconfigured behavior today, so nothing changes for San Miguel/Cavite until BPLO deliberately reconfigures), `accepts_gcash`/`gcash_number`/`gcash_name`, `accepts_bank_transfer`/`bank_name`/`bank_account_number`/`bank_account_name`, `accepts_online_portal`/`online_portal_url` -- all defaulting off/blank otherwise, same "no generic fallback for a real-world-specific fact" reasoning as `mayor_name`/`treasurer_name`. No new RLS policy -- migration 0027's existing general "BPLO can update their own LGU's settings" policy already covers all ten columns.

**`src/lib/payment-methods.ts`** (new, shared): `getEnabledPaymentChannels(lgu)` turns an `LguDisplay` into a `PaymentChannel[]` (one entry per *actually usable* channel -- GCash only appears if both `acceptsGcash` and a real `gcashNumber` are set, so a half-configured toggle can't silently produce a broken instruction), each carrying a short SMS-length detail and a fuller email-length detail plus whether it needs a proof upload. `formatPaymentChannelsForSms()`/`formatPaymentChannelsForEmailHtml()` are the two renderers, used identically by both the assessment-finalized notification (`bplo/actions.ts`) and the applicant status page (`status/[reference]/page.tsx`) -- one shared derivation so the two can never describe the payment options differently. Falls back to `"Contact the BPLO office to arrange payment."` if an LGU somehow has zero channels configured (shouldn't happen given `accepts_cash_counter` defaults true, but a real LGU could turn everything off).

**`finalizeAssessment`** (`bplo/actions.ts`): the assessment-finalized SMS now ends with `formatPaymentChannelsForSms(paymentChannels)` instead of the hardcoded Treasurer's-Office line; the Order of Payment email gets an HTML list of every enabled channel's full detail appended after the PDF-attachment paragraph. **Deliberately not added to the Order of Payment PDF itself this pass** -- `order-of-payment-pdf.ts` lays out a single fixed-height Letter page with the signature block anchored at a hardcoded `sigY`, not a value computed from how much content precedes it; inserting a variable-length payment-methods block without reworking that whole vertical-flow calculation risks silently overlapping the signature block or running off the page. Flagged here rather than silently skipped -- a reasonable follow-up if wanted, but a separately-scoped layout change, not something to risk breaking a document real Treasury staff already rely on for this pass.

**Settings UI**: a new "Accepted Payment Methods" section (`payment-methods.tsx`, `Documents & Alerts` group, right after Order of Payment Details) -- the one piece of real client interactivity is each toggle revealing its own detail fields only once checked (the project owner's own explicit ask: "if switch on, another field pop up"), the same "client JS only where the feature genuinely needs it, plain server-action form otherwise" pattern as `PermitNumberFormatCard`/`AssessmentLineItems`. `updatePaymentMethods` (`settings/actions.ts`) is the authoritative validation -- won't let GCash/Bank Transfer/Online Portal be turned on without their required detail fields (a GCash number, a bank name + account number, a URL that at least starts with `http(s)://`), independent of whatever the client-side Save-button-disable already caught.

**Staff document visibility** (`payment-queue.tsx`): `AwaitingPaymentSection`'s per-application card is now its own async `PaymentQueueRow` sub-component (was inline in a `.map()`, which can't `await` per-row) so it can fetch and show that application's documents via the same `getApplicationDocuments`/`DocumentList` pattern `InitialReviewCard` already established -- a GCash/Bank Transfer screenshot uploaded via the status page now actually shows up somewhere Treasury/BPLO will see it before recording payment, closing the gap the discovery above surfaced.

**Applicant status page**: a new `PendingPaymentSection` wraps the existing `pending_payment` branch -- fetches the LGU's enabled channels and renders a "How to pay" card listing each one's full detail (right on the page, not just in the one-time SMS/email), then renders `UploadCard` with a payment-specific prompt/default label ("Paid via GCash, Bank Transfer, or online? Upload your receipt or screenshot here...") whenever at least one enabled channel needs a proof upload -- falling back to the original generic wording when the LGU is cash-only, since there's nothing to prove in that case. `UploadCard` itself gained two new optional props (`promptOverride`/`defaultLabelOverride`) rather than a second component, since the underlying upload mechanism is identical either way.

**Verified against real production data and logic, not just types**: RLS confirmed via a rolled-back transaction -- a real BPLO session at San Miguel can write all ten new columns, with production's real (still cash-only-default) config confirmed untouched afterward. `getEnabledPaymentChannels`/`formatPaymentChannelsForSms`/`formatPaymentChannelsForEmailHtml` verified via a temporary fixture route (deleted immediately after) against four scenarios: cash-only, all four channels configured, GCash enabled with a missing number (correctly degrades to showing nothing for that channel rather than a broken instruction), and nothing configured at all (correctly falls back to the generic contact-BPLO message). The Settings card's toggle-reveal and Save-disable-until-valid behavior confirmed interactively via a temporary fixture (deleted immediately after) -- checking GCash correctly revealed its two fields with Save disabled, typing a number correctly re-enabled it. The new `DocumentList` addition to the payment queue confirmed end-to-end via a temporary fixture (deleted immediately after): a fully synthetic application with a real `documents` row correctly round-tripped through `getApplicationDocuments`/`getSignedDocumentUrl`/`DocumentList`, rendering the uploaded document's label -- all synthetic rows deleted afterward, confirmed zero leftover data in production. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` on every touched file, and a full `next build` all ran clean, and the real `/dashboard/settings`, `/dashboard/treasury`, and `/status/[reference]` routes were confirmed to compile and render with zero server errors.

**Same-day follow-up: a real bug, reported live by the project owner** ("I clicked the box for all of them, but when I clicked save, the checked box was removed and the save button is highlighted again, making it look like it was never saved"). Confirmed against production first, before touching any code: the database write was actually completely correct (`accepts_gcash`/`accepts_bank_transfer`/`accepts_online_portal` all `true`, with the right GCash/bank/portal details saved) -- this was a pure client-side display bug, not data loss, but a real and confusing one (it looked exactly like the Save had silently failed).

**Root cause, confirmed by direct reproduction rather than guessed at**: React 19 resets a `<form>`'s own DOM elements after a successful `action={fn}` submit -- documented behavior, but a real trap for a controlled input whose value lives in a component's own `useState` rather than the DOM. Built a minimal throwaway repro (a client component with one controlled checkbox inside a form pointed at a trivial server action, deleted immediately after): checking the box, then submitting, left the checkbox visually unchecked afterward even though the component's own state variable was still (correctly) `true` -- proven directly by a conditionally-rendered element driven by that exact same state variable staying visible the whole time. A second repro confirmed a controlled `<select>` has the identical problem (a changed selection silently reverts to its original value after a successful submit) while a plain controlled `<input type="text">` does **not** -- meaning `PermitNumberFormatCard` (section 7vv, shipped the same day, has two `<select>`s for year/counter digit width) carried this exact same latent bug, not yet reported only because nobody had tested a save-then-look-again cycle on it specifically.

**The fix, verified before applying to the real components**: force a clean remount whenever the underlying saved data actually changes, via a `key` prop derived from every field the component owns, passed at the call site in `settings/page.tsx` (e.g. `key={[lgu.acceptsCashCounter, lgu.acceptsGcash, ...].join("|")}` for `PaymentMethodsCard`, `key={`${prefix}|${yearDigits}|${counterDigits}`}` for `PermitNumberFormatCard`). Confirmed the mechanism directly first, in an extended version of the same repro (a real server action + `revalidatePath`, a server-rendered key derived from persisted state): after applying the identical `key` pattern, the checkbox correctly showed checked after a real save-and-refresh cycle, matching the server's own truth. Applied the same fix to both real components -- `PaymentMethodsCard` (this section) and `PermitNumberFormatCard` (section 7vv), the one other component in this codebase using the same vulnerable pattern (a controlled checkbox/select bound to `useState`, submitted through a plain `<form action={fn}>` without a remount-forcing key). Both components' own doc comments now explain why the caller's `key` prop is load-bearing, not decorative, so a future edit that drops it doesn't quietly reintroduce this bug. No other Settings form in this codebase uses this same pattern -- every other toggle here uses a hidden-input-plus-button-click shape (e.g. `setAutomatedAssessmentEnabled`) that never had persistent client state to desync from in the first place.

Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and a full `next build`, all clean, plus confirming `/dashboard/settings` still compiles and renders with zero server errors after the fix.

---

## 7yy. Self-service department management on `/admin` (2026-08-19)

The project owner asked directly how approving departments (Engineering, MHO, MPDO, BFP, MENRO for San Miguel) are managed, and what an LGU would do to add one. Researched before answering, not guessed: `lgu_departments` could only ever be written by a platform admin (RLS, migration 0018), and in practice the *only* code path that ever inserted into it was `createLguClient` -- meaning a department could only be set **once, at the moment a new LGU is first onboarded**. There was no way to add, rename, or deactivate a department for a client that already exists, anywhere in the product -- not even for the platform admin. The project owner's own reaction, correctly: "this isn't a scalable design... at the very least, in the admin login, I should be able to add it."

**Deliberately scoped to `/admin` only, not also a BPLO-facing Settings section** -- the project owner's own explicit ask ("at the very least, in the admin login"), not a guess at wanting the fuller barangays-style self-service version too. RLS already restricted this to platform admins regardless (migration 0018's `lgu_departments` policy is unchanged), so no new migration was needed -- only new UI and two new server actions.

**`addLguDepartment`/`setDepartmentActive`** (`admin/actions.ts`): a new "Manage departments" `<details>` disclosure per client row on `/admin`, matching the existing "Embed code" disclosure's own pattern -- lists every department (active shown normally, inactive shown struck-through and grayed, matching the Regulatory Fees Settings section's own established convention for this exact visual shape) each with a Deactivate/Reactivate toggle button, plus an "Add department" inline form. Soft-delete only (`is_active`), never a hard delete -- a department that already has real `department_reviews` history must keep showing correctly on those historical rows, same standing convention as every other soft-deletable row in this schema.

**A real constraint discovered and handled, not assumed away**: `lgu_departments` has a `unique(lgu_id, name)` constraint -- confirmed directly that a naive re-insert of a previously-deactivated department's exact name hits it (`23505 duplicate key`, verified via a rolled-back SQL transaction before writing any app code). `addLguDepartment` checks for an existing row (case-insensitive) first: a name that's already active is a safe no-op (matching `addBarangays`' own "re-submitting the same thing twice doesn't error" convention), a name that exists but is inactive gets automatically **reactivated** instead of insert-and-collide -- a one-step recovery rather than making the admin separately scroll down and find the right Deactivate/Reactivate toggle themselves.

**The mid-round behavior question was already answered by 7qq's earlier fix, not reopened here**: adding or reactivating a department never reaches back into an application already sitting in `pending_dept_review` -- it only affects the *next* review round a given application opens (a fresh submission, or a resubmission). Stated directly in the new section's own UI copy, not left implicit, since it's exactly the kind of behavior a platform admin would otherwise reasonably (and wrongly) assume happens automatically and immediately.

**No disconnect with Add Staff, confirmed by tracing the code rather than assumed**: the Settings "Add/Remove Staff" department `<select>` (`staff-management.tsx`) already re-queries `lgu_departments` live, scoped to the signed-in BPLO's own LGU, on every page load -- any row added here shows up there immediately, with zero additional wiring needed.

**Verified against real production data and logic, not just types**: RLS confirmed via two rolled-back transactions -- the real platform admin session can insert a department at San Miguel, while a session with no matching `platform_admins`/`staff_users` row at all is correctly denied (`42501`, RLS violation). The unique-constraint collision this feature exists to handle was independently reproduced first (confirming the fix is solving a real, not hypothetical, problem) before writing `addLguDepartment`'s own check-then-reactivate logic. That logic was then verified end-to-end via a temporary fixture route (deleted immediately after) replicating the exact steps via service-role against a fully synthetic LGU: a fresh add inserts a row, adding the identical active name again correctly no-ops, deactivating flips the flag, and adding the same name back in a **different case** (`"zoning"` vs. the stored `"Zoning"`) while inactive correctly reactivates the *same* row (proving the case-insensitive match) rather than colliding with the unique constraint -- all synthetic rows deleted afterward, confirmed zero leftover data in production. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and a full `next build` all ran clean, and the real `/admin` route was confirmed to compile and redirect cleanly with zero server errors.

---

## 7zz. SMS usage counter (2026-08-19)

The project owner's own arrangement: 1000 free SMS/month per municipality, no carryover, Php 0.55 each over that -- asked for a counter, resetting monthly, with a small history of previous months underneath. Confirmed a real placement fork before building rather than guessing: the request said "inside the settings," but the actual reasoning (free-tier/billing exposure) pointed more naturally at the platform admin's own `/admin` view across every client. Built both -- `/admin` (every client, for the project owner's own billing visibility) and each LGU's own Settings (so that municipality's BPLO can see their own usage too), per the project owner's own confirmed choice.

**A real, significant gap found before writing the counter itself, not after**: `sendOtpCode(phone)` -- OTP is very likely the single largest source of real SMS volume in this whole app (every login, renewal, and status re-verification sends one) -- called Semaphore directly and never logged anything to `notifications_log` at all, and had no LGU attached anywhere in its own data model (`otp_codes` has no `lgu_id` column, and the function itself took no LGU parameter). A counter built only on top of what was already tracked (`notifyApplicantSms`/`notifyStaffSms`, the status-update/reminder/staff-alert traffic) would have silently missed the majority of real volume -- actively misleading for a feature whose entire purpose is billing accuracy. Fixing this was a precondition for the feature to mean anything, not a nice-to-have.

**Migration `0054_notifications_log_lgu_id.sql`** (live in production): a new `notifications_log.lgu_id`, denormalized directly onto the row at insert time rather than derived later via `application_id` -- several real notification types have always had `application_id = null` (OTP sends, the platform admin's staff-welcome email), which a join-based derivation would silently miss for exactly the rows this feature most needs. Two new, additive SELECT policies -- `notifications_log` has had RLS enabled with zero policies since migration 0001 (confirmed by design in section 7q's own follow-up, since writes only ever happen via `createServiceClient()`) -- one for BPLO at their own LGU (mirrors `audit_log`'s existing BPLO-only pattern), one for any active platform admin across every LGU. Neither widens who can *write*.

**`notifications.ts`**: the private `log()` helper is now exported as `logNotification()` and takes an optional `lguId` -- `notifyApplicantSms`/`notifyStaffSms` (which already receive `lguId` as a parameter) now pass it through; email logging is untouched (the counter only ever cares about `channel = 'sms'`, so email rows don't need it).

**`otp.ts`**: `sendOtpCode(phone)` -> `sendOtpCode(phone, lguId)`, now logging through the exact same `logNotification()` path as every other real SMS type, best-effort and never throwing (a logging failure must never block a real OTP from reaching someone). **Deliberately did not also fix OTP's separate, unrelated branding gap** (it never uses `resolveSmsIdentity()`'s LGU-aware custom Sender Name/prefix logic, unlike every other SMS type) -- flagged here rather than silently bundled in, since OTP is the single highest-stakes SMS type in this app and this pass was about counting, not changing what the message looks like. All three real call sites updated to supply a real `lguId`: `send-otp/route.ts` (a brand-new applicant, no business yet -- resolves via `resolveLguId(host)`, the same technique `submit-application`/`lookup-license` already use) and `send-renewal-otp/route.ts`/`send-status-otp/route.ts` (an existing business/application already known -- resolves directly from that row's own `lgu_id`, more precise than a host-header guess).

**`src/lib/sms-usage.ts`** (new, shared): `getCurrentMonthSmsCount()` (a compact single-month count, for `/admin`'s per-client row) and `getSmsUsageHistory()` (current month + N previous, for both full displays) -- both count only `channel = 'sms'` and `status = 'sent'` (a `'failed'` row means `sendSms()` itself threw before Semaphore ever accepted the message, so it was never actually billed). Month boundaries use the exact same "shift `now` by +8h, read the shifted instant's UTC getters as Manila-local calendar fields" technique `department-reminders/route.ts` already established, extended here to compute real UTC instant boundaries for the query (shifting back by -8h) rather than just a day-of-week check.

**Settings** (`sms-usage-card.tsx`, a new "SMS Usage" section in Documents & Alerts, right before SMS Notifications): current month shown prominently (count, remaining-or-overage, and an estimated peso cost when over), with the previous 5 months listed below -- matching the project owner's own literal description ("should show below the record for the previous month"). **`/admin`**: a compact "SMS this month: X / 1,000 free" summary per client row (matching the existing app/business-count convention already shown there), expandable via the same `<details>` disclosure pattern as Manage Departments/Embed code, revealing the same previous-months history.

**Verified against real production data and logic, not just types**: RLS confirmed two ways -- a real BPLO/platform-admin session at San Miguel correctly sees a synthetic row inserted directly (bypassing the app), and a session with no matching `staff_users`/`platform_admins` row at all is correctly denied (confirmed via a rolled-back transaction for the denial case; the visibility case used a real, briefly-committed synthetic row, deleted immediately after, since RLS SELECT behavior can't be observed inside a transaction that's about to roll back the very row being checked). The counting/month-bucketing logic verified end-to-end via a temporary fixture route (deleted immediately after): synthetic rows spanning three real calendar months (this month, last month, two months ago), plus a failed SMS and a sent email in the current month specifically to prove both get correctly excluded -- every month's count and the current-month total matched expectations exactly, with zero leftover data confirmed afterward. The overage math verified via a second temporary fixture (1,105 synthetic SMS in the current month): correctly computed 105 over the free limit at exactly ₱57.75 (105 × ₱0.55), with the real card's own exact JSX/styling rendered and read back to confirm the numbers display correctly, not just that the underlying function returns them. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and a full `next build` all ran clean, and the real `/dashboard/settings` and `/admin` routes were confirmed to compile and redirect cleanly with zero server errors (one transient, unrelated dev-environment error on the landing page during a concurrent test-fixture write was independently confirmed not to have touched production data, and did not recur).

---

## 7a1. Nature of Business: searchable instead of a plain dropdown (2026-08-19)

The project owner's own live-testing report: "I can only type one letter" -- Nature of Business is a native `<select>` with ~220 options, and a native select only ever supports the browser's own built-in typeahead (jump to whichever option starts with the last key pressed, resetting almost immediately), not a real substring search. With 220 entries, that's genuinely painful to use.

**Built as a real, reusable `FieldDescriptor` kind (`"searchable-select"`), not a one-off hack tied to this specific field** -- `ApplyPageClient.tsx`'s new `SearchableSelect` component is a plain type-to-filter combobox: typing filters the option list by substring (case-insensitive) anywhere in the text, not just the start; clicking or arrow-key-navigating-then-Enter selects; Escape or clicking away reverts the visible text back to whatever's actually selected rather than leaving a half-typed, unselected state. `withDynamicOptions()` (the existing per-LGU option-swap mechanism, section 7o) already treats it identically to a plain `select` for override purposes, so this stays compatible with a future LGU's own nature-of-business list the same way the field already was. Deliberately still resolves to one exact string from the option list on selection -- typing itself never becomes the stored value, since `application-form-logic.ts`'s conditional show/hide rules pattern-match on exact option text (e.g. `natureOfBusiness === "Billiard Hall"`).

**A real bug found live during verification, not by inspection**: after selecting an option, clicking the (already-focused) input again to search for something else did nothing -- the dropdown never reopened. Root cause: selecting an option uses `onMouseDown` + `preventDefault()` (needed so the browser's default focus-shifting behavior doesn't blur the input and close the dropdown before the click registers), which means the input never actually loses focus during selection -- so a second click on it doesn't fire a *new* `onFocus` event, since focus never changed. Fixed by also opening on plain `onClick`, not just `onFocus` -- covers both "focusing in from a different element" and "clicking an input that was already focused."

**Verified interactively against the real component, not a re-implementation** -- since `SearchableSelect` has no session/auth dependency at all (unlike the rest of `/apply`'s OTP-gated later screens), it was temporarily exported, exercised directly in a fixture with the real 220-entry `NATURE_OF_BUSINESS_OPTIONS` list, then reverted: substring filtering confirmed correct (typing "comp" matched "Computer Repair," "Computer/Gadget Store," "Finance Company," etc. -- anywhere-in-string, not just prefix); mouse selection confirmed (updates the bound value, closes the dropdown); the re-click-to-reopen bug above was caught and fixed live during this same pass; the "no matches" state, Escape-reverts-without-selecting, and full keyboard navigation (arrow keys move the highlighted row, Enter selects it) were all confirmed against the real component afterward. `tsc`/`eslint`/a full `next build` all ran clean, and the real, unmodified `/apply` route was confirmed to compile and render with zero server errors.

---

## 7a2. Vercel function region: Virginia -> Tokyo, to sit next to the database (2026-08-19)

The project owner asked to verify a leftover item from the 23-finding pipeline audit (section 7qq): the theory that clicking between dashboard tabs/sections feels slow partly because the server runs "in Virginia instead of Singapore." Checked both platforms directly rather than trusting the audit's own memory of itself:

- **Vercel (where every Server Component/Server Action actually runs)**: `iad1` -- Washington, D.C. area, Virginia, US East. Confirmed from a live production deployment's own `regions` field. No `vercel.json`/route-level region override existed anywhere in the codebase before this, so every function used this project-level default.
- **Supabase (the database)**: `ap-northeast-1` -- Tokyo, Japan, not Singapore. Confirmed directly via the project's own metadata.

**The audit's diagnosis was directionally right but its own specifics were off, and the real problem is actually one layer worse than "far from the Philippines"**: the function and the database aren't just each far from Manila, they're far from *each other* -- every Supabase call a Server Component makes has to cross the Pacific from Virginia to Tokyo and back, and this app's dashboard pages routinely make several such calls *sequentially* per page load (LGU lookup, applications, departments, review rounds), so that penalty compounds rather than paying once.

**Fix applied**: `vercel.json` gained a project-level `"regions": ["hnd1"]` (Vercel's Tokyo region) -- confirmed against Vercel's own current docs (`search_vercel_documentation`, not assumed from training data, matching this project's standing AGENTS.md caution about this stack) that a top-level `regions` array is the documented way to set the default execution region for every function in a project. This collapses the function<->database leg (paid multiple times per page) from a transpacific US-East-Coast round trip down to a same-metro hop, leaving only a single Manila<->Tokyo leg for the user-facing request instead of a Manila<->Virginia<->Tokyo<->Virginia<->Manila chain.

**Deliberately not also moving the database to Singapore in this pass** -- discussed directly with the project owner first: Supabase has no in-place region change, only create-a-new-project-and-migrate, and while the actual data footprint here is small enough to make that low-risk (1,181 businesses, 8 applications, 44 documents/~20MB, 13,555 static permit_history rows, 3 real staff/admin logins at the time this was checked), it's still a real cutover for a live pilot client's production data. The project owner's own choice was to ship the free, reversible Vercel-region change first and observe whether that alone meaningfully improves things before committing to a database migration.

---

## 7a3. Full-system QA sweep, then the 4 findings fixed (2026-08-19/20)

The project owner asked for a genuinely broad functional pass -- not a code review, a behavior review: run realistic applications through every real branch (new/renewal, approve/reject/request-info at every stage including per-department, Automated Assessment on/off, and every setting shipped this same day -- payment methods, CEDULA, barangay clearance, business tax installments, the Engineering fee) and report what's actually broken, missing, or worth improving, working autonomously and reporting back rather than checking in along the way.

**Built a fully synthetic, disposable test municipality** ("QA Test Municipality," subdomain `qatest`) rather than testing against San Miguel or Cavite -- cloned San Miguel's entire real fee catalog (126 `fee_rules`, 139 brackets, all 5 departments, all 49 barangays) via SQL id-remapping so every scenario ran against a realistic, fully-configured LGU without any risk to real client data. Every applicant-facing "session" (owner rows) was created with `phone = null, email = null` deliberately -- since `owners.phone` has no NOT NULL constraint, this made every notification code path a guaranteed no-op if it happened to run for real, closing off any chance of a real Semaphore SMS charge or a stray email during a broad, many-scenario sweep. Confirmed afterward: the SMS usage counter for the test LGU stayed at zero the entire pass.

**Verification technique**: every genuinely pure/session-independent function (`computeApplicationFees`, `openDepartmentReviewRound`, `createInfoRequest`, `resolveOpenInfoRequests`, `getEnabledPaymentChannels`, `getLbtCategoryOptions`, `computeRevenueReport`, the PDF generators, ...) was called directly via a temporary, unauthenticated `/api/qa-harness` route -- the same fixture-route technique this project has used since section 7d, just consolidated into one reusable route instead of a new one-off per check. Every session-gated action (`submitInitialReview`, `finalizeAssessment`, `submitDepartmentDecision`, `recordPayment`, `signPermit`, ...) can't be called directly (each needs a real Google-OAuth staff session, which can't be driven in this environment) -- these were replicated as direct SQL writes matching each action file's exact current logic, re-read from source immediately before replicating it rather than trusted from memory. 13 scenarios run end to end; 2 carried all the way through to a released, signed permit.

**Two bugs were caught in the test harness itself, not the product** -- wrong argument counts calling `areAllDepartmentsCleared`/`getEngineeringAssessedAmount`/`getLbtCategoryOptions` (each takes one parameter, not a `(supabase, id)` pair), which initially made real, correct functions look broken. Caught by cross-checking against a raw debug query before trusting the harness's own output, rather than reporting a false positive.

**Four real findings, all confirmed by direct reproduction, not inferred from reading code:**

1. **[High, live in production] Renewal applications with Business Tax Payment = "Bi-Annually" could never be finalized.** `finalizeAssessment` (`bplo/actions.ts`) writes `mode_of_payment: business.business_tax_payment` -- "Bi-Annually" verbatim for a renewal that chose it (the installment-payments feature, section 7ww, shipped the same day this bug was found). But `applications.mode_of_payment`'s own CHECK constraint (migration 0039, predates 7ww) still only allowed the OLD vocabulary (`'Annual', 'Semi-Annual', 'Quarterly'`) -- 7ww's own CLAUDE.md text said "the canonical stored value is now 'Bi-Annually' everywhere," but the database schema itself was never updated to match what the code actually started writing. Reproduced directly: `update applications set mode_of_payment='Bi-Annually'` threw `23514 violates check constraint`, 100% reproducible. Quarterly was unaffected (already in the old allowed list) -- only Bi-Annually was broken, and completely: BPLO would see a raw constraint-violation error the instant they tried to finalize, with the application stuck at `pending_bplo_assessment` permanently.

   **Fixed** (migration `0055_fix_mode_of_payment_check.sql`, applied directly to production): widened the constraint to `('Annual', 'Bi-Annually', 'Quarterly')`. `'Semi-Annual'` is dropped outright, not kept alongside the new value -- confirmed first that zero real rows use it (only `null` and `'Annual'` exist in production today), so there was nothing to preserve compatibility for; keeping a permanently-dead allowed value would just reintroduce the same "schema says one thing, code says another" drift this migration exists to close. Verified with a rolled-back transaction: `update ... set mode_of_payment='Bi-Annually'` now succeeds.

2. **[Medium] Reopening a REJECTED application restored the wrong stage.** `submitInitialReview`'s `decision === "rejected"` branch sets `status: "archived"` directly but never wrote `archived_from_status` -- confirmed directly (a synthetic rejected app had `archived_from_status: null`). `reopenApplication`'s fallback (`archived_from_status ?? "returned_to_applicant"`) then restored the WRONG stage on reopen: `returned_to_applicant` instead of `pending_bplo_initial`, where the application actually came from. Only the manual `archiveApplication` path (not the Reject decision) stamped this correctly. Real trigger: BPLO rejects by mistake, or the applicant clarifies in person and BPLO wants a second look.

   **Fixed** (`bplo/actions.ts`): the reject branch's own `applications` UPDATE now also sets `archived_from_status: "pending_bplo_initial"` whenever `newStatus === "archived"`, matching exactly what `archiveApplication` already does for every other transition into `archived`. Verified end to end with a synthetic application: rejected (confirmed `archived_from_status: "pending_bplo_initial"`), then reopened (confirmed it correctly restores to `pending_bplo_initial`, not `returned_to_applicant`).

3. **[Medium] Stats & Reports' "Actual Permit" breakdown mislabeled the essential-commodity discount.** `revenue-report.ts`'s per-application sub-breakdown folded the discount's own `subCategory` into "Other regulatory fees" (`l.subCategory === "other_regulatory" || l.subCategory === "discount"`) -- a discount against Local Business Tax has nothing to do with regulatory fees. Grand totals were always correct (the discount landed in the `actual_permit` bucket total either way); only the LBT-vs-other-regulatory *split* was wrong, in both directions at once -- LBT read too high (ungrossed against its own discount), Other regulatory read too low (absorbing a discount it never charged). Confirmed with real numbers: a synthetic ₱1,000 LBT line with a real ₱500 discount showed "Local Business Tax: ₱1,000" and implicitly "-₱500" buried in the wrong bucket before the fix.

   **Fixed**: the discount now nets against "Local Business Tax" in the breakdown instead, matching how `fee-engine.ts` itself already treats the discount -- a reduction of the LBT line, not a regulatory adjustment. Verified directly: the same synthetic ₱1,000 LBT / ₱500 discount case now shows "Local Business Tax: ₱500" (net), "Other regulatory fees: ₱0".

4. **[Low] Two department-review checks failed silently on a genuine query error.** `areAllDepartmentsCleared` and `getEngineeringAssessedAmount` (`review-workflow.ts`) both destructured only `{ data }` from their Supabase queries, never checking `error` -- a real transient failure (network blip, connection exhaustion) would look identical to "nothing cleared yet" / "no amount entered," with zero error trail. Fails safe (blocks progress rather than wrongly advancing or undercharging), but is exactly the kind of unexplained-stuck-application support case this project has been burned by twice before in unrelated places (`owners`' missing RLS policy, section 7q; `current_staff()`'s row-ordering bug, section 7u) -- an app-layer/DB-layer answer silently drifting from what actually happened.

   **Fixed**: both functions now check `error` on every query and throw a real error instead of silently returning `false`/`null`. One nuance handled deliberately, not blindly: `getEngineeringAssessedAmount` is also called from `bplo/page.tsx`'s `AssessmentCard` -- a live preview rendered per-application in a list with no per-card error boundary, where a single transient blip throwing would take down the whole Assessment-review tab instead of just that one card's Engineering line. That call site now wraps the call in its own `try/catch`, logging and degrading to `null` (its original behavior) on failure -- so the underlying library function is honest about real errors everywhere, while the one read-only preview render stays exactly as resilient as it was before. `finalizeAssessment`'s own call (a one-shot mutation, no existing try/catch anywhere else in that function either) is left to throw for real, which is the desired fix. Verified with a synthetic application (one approved Engineering review, `assessed_amount: 777`): both functions still return the correct values (`true` / `777`) after the fix -- confirmed no regression on the normal path, only the error path changed.

**All four fixes verified against real/synthetic data before being considered done, not just type-checked** -- a rolled-back transaction for the migration, and temporary fixture-route calls for the three code fixes, each using a fresh synthetic row created and deleted within the same check, with production's real counts (San Miguel: 1,181 businesses, 8 applications, 126 fee_rules) confirmed unchanged before and after every step of this whole pass. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` on every touched file, and a full `next build` all ran clean at the end.

---

## 7a4. Critical cross-tenant RLS gap on `department_reviews`, fixed during a full-system audit (2026-08-20)

A full audit (UI/design, mobile, code quality, security, ops readiness, performance, run in five parallel passes) found one **critical** issue: `department_reviews`' own "department scoped access" policy (migration 0002) matched purely on department NAME (`'Engineering' = 'Engineering'`), with no join back to `applications.lgu_id` -- unlike every other policy on this table (`"bplo full access"`, `"treasury and mayor can view"`, migration 0047), which both correctly join through to `applications.lgu_id`. Confirmed directly against production before fixing: San Miguel and the Demo Account LGU both use standard department names that collide (Engineering, MENRO, MHO); a genuine `department`-role account at either LGU could read/write the *other* LGU's `department_reviews` rows for a same-named department -- including `assessed_amount` (the Engineering-typed Building Permit Fee, section 7aa), which feeds directly into what a real applicant gets charged. The Demo Account's own Engineering reviewer had never signed in at audit time (`auth_user_id null`), so this wasn't caught mid-exploit by a live session -- but the gap was real, live, and would have activated the moment that account (or any future colliding-named department account) signed in.

**Fixed** (migration `0056_fix_department_scoped_rls_cross_tenant.sql`, applied directly to production): added the identical `review_rounds`/`applications` join + `s.lgu_id = a.lgu_id` check every other policy on this table already uses (migration 0047 is the direct template) -- same "widen/fix one policy at a time, don't touch the others" caution this schema's RLS history already establishes throughout.

**Verified against real production data before and after**: confirmed via `pg_policies` that the fixed policy's `qual` now includes the join; re-ran `get_advisors` (security) afterward and confirmed no new findings were introduced. Verified no regression with a rolled-back transaction under San Miguel's own real, active Engineering department-reviewer session (`auth_user_id d7864521-328a-4c45-bc12-9354df4e8f2b`) -- correctly still sees exactly its own 8 real `department_reviews` rows post-fix, same count as before the fix, confirming legitimate same-LGU access is untouched. A true live cross-tenant negative test (two different LGUs' colliding-named accounts both actually signed in) wasn't possible against real data since only one of the two colliding accounts has ever authenticated -- the fix's correctness instead rests on it being byte-for-byte the same join shape as migration 0047's already-proven policy on this identical table.

---

## 7a5. OTP SMS endpoints rate-limited by IP address (2026-08-20, same full-system audit)

The audit's own explicit question ("could someone spam an SMS-sending endpoint and run up a real bill?") came back yes: `send-otp/route.ts`'s only abuse protection was a 30-second cooldown per phone number -- nothing stopped one visitor from working through many different numbers, each a real billed Semaphore SMS to a real third party who never asked for one. Two of the three related sign-in paths (renewal-by-permit-number, status-page re-verification) were already meaningfully safer, since they resolve the phone number server-side rather than trusting whatever the visitor typed -- this pass is about the plain phone-entry path (and its two siblings, for defense in depth) specifically.

**`src/lib/rate-limit.ts`** (new) + migration `0057_otp_rate_limit_events.sql` (live in production): a per-IP throttle, 5 sends per 10 minutes, self-cleaning (old rows pruned as part of the check itself, no separate cron needed). Applied to all three OTP-send routes (`send-otp`, `send-renewal-otp`, `send-status-otp`) alongside the existing per-phone-number cooldown, not instead of it -- the two protect against different abuse shapes (one visitor cycling through many numbers vs. one number being hit repeatedly).

**Verified**: `tsc`, `eslint`, and a full `next build` all clean. Live-checked against the actual dev server (not just read the code) -- confirmed via the real rendered page and console/network inspection that the throttle activates and the client shows a real, specific error message rather than a generic failure. Further live verification deliberately stopped short of actually exhausting the limit against a real phone number, to avoid spending a real Semaphore SMS credit purely for the sake of the test, matching this project's own standing rule about not spending real notification credits during verification passes.

---

## 7a6. Text-overlap bug fixed across all 4 PDF generators (2026-08-20, same audit)

Three of this project's four PDF generators (`permit-pdf.ts`, `print-certificate.ts`, `order-of-payment-pdf.ts`) advanced each label/value row by a fixed height regardless of how many lines the actual value wrapped to -- a business name or address long enough to wrap onto a second line would visually overlap the very next row on the signed permit, the pre-signature printable certificate, or the Order of Payment slip. Nothing on the applicant form limits how long a business name can be, so this was never a rare edge case, only a matter of when a real filing would trigger it. A fourth, newer generator (`application-form-pdf.ts`) had already solved this correctly, but the fix was never carried back to the three older ones -- exactly the failure mode of four files duplicating the same row-layout logic instead of sharing it.

**Fixed**: extracted the already-correct row-height calculation into a new shared `src/lib/pdf-doc-utils.ts`, and pointed all four generators at it (including `application-form-pdf.ts` itself, for full consistency going forward, not just the three that were actually broken).

**Verified by actually rendering a real PDF with a long, wrapping business name** (a temporary script, deleted after use, matching this project's own established verification convention for every other PDF-generation change) -- confirmed the bug reproduced pre-fix (a 2-line/28pt-tall row only advancing 26pt) and confirmed clean, non-overlapping output post-fix. `tsc`/`eslint`/a full `next build` all clean.

---

## 7a7. A lint gate finally runs before every build, plus a narrow cron-recipient gap closed (2026-08-20, same audit)

Next.js 16 stopped running ESLint automatically as part of `next build` -- confirmed directly, not assumed -- meaning a real lint error could reach a live production deploy with nothing in the pipeline failing. Found and fixed the one real (pre-existing, low-risk, already-understood) error this had let through: `theme-toggle.tsx`'s `set-state-in-effect` pattern, which had a comment already acknowledging the tradeoff but only actually covered one line of it -- restructured to a single `setState` call so the disable comment genuinely covers the whole thing. Also added `ignoreRestSiblings` to the ESLint config for a destructure-to-omit idiom (`const { x, ...rest } = obj`) already used consistently elsewhere in this codebase, which was tripping `no-unused-vars` as a false positive rather than a real issue.

**Fixed**: a `prebuild` script (npm's own lifecycle hook, honored automatically by Vercel's default build command) runs `eslint .` before `next build` ever starts, so this gap can't reopen silently for any future change either.

**Also closed in the same pass**: the department-reminder cron (`department-reminders/route.ts`) didn't exclude the platform admin's "View as" proxy accounts from its recipient list, the same exclusion already applied at three other similar call sites (its email is a synthetic address never meant to actually receive mail) -- a narrow, no-real-impact-yet gap, closed for consistency rather than because it had caused a real problem.

**Verified**: `tsc`, `eslint` (confirmed clean after the fix, and confirmed the lint gate genuinely fires as part of `npm run build`, not just that the script exists), and a full `next build`, all clean.

---

## 7a8. Mobile UX and accessibility gaps fixed on every applicant-facing page (2026-08-20, same audit)

The audit's highest-severity findings were concentrated here, on purpose -- this is the one surface real, non-technical applicants on real (often older, lower-end) phones actually use, tested live at real narrow viewports (down to 375px) rather than a resized desktop browser.

- **Critical, accessibility**: every one of the ~40 form labels (plus the phone/OTP fields on every screen) was visually next to its input but not programmatically connected to it -- confirmed live, a screen reader announced nothing when a field was focused. Fixed at the shared field-rendering level (`Field`, `renderField()`, `SearchableSelect`) so every field gained a real `id`/`htmlFor` pairing at once, plus the hand-written fields (permit lookup, phone, OTP, name/email/gender) wired individually.
- **Critical, mobile**: touch targets were well under the ~44px comfortable minimum (measured live: the Send Code button was 77×32px) -- raised across the shared input/button styling used throughout the form, one change fixing it everywhere.
- **High, mobile**: the signature pad's drawing coordinates didn't scale by the ratio of the canvas's internal resolution to its displayed CSS size, so a signature drawn on a phone narrower than the canvas's internal 560px visibly compressed into the left half of the box. Fixed with a coordinate-scaling correction in the pad's own pointer-handling code.
- **Medium, mobile** (the project owner's own direct question): a dropped connection, a killed background tab, or an accidental reload lost the entire in-progress ~40-field form, which had only ever lived in memory. Now periodically autosaves to the browser's own local storage (keyed to the verified session) and restores on reload.
- **Medium, mobile**: the phone number field only checked for non-empty before spending a real, billed OTP SMS -- now checked against a real Philippine mobile format (11 digits, starts with 09) first.
- **Medium, accessibility**: the Business Activity checkbox group had no grouping markup at all -- now a real `<fieldset>`/`<legend>`, so a screen reader announces it as a related set of options.
- **Low, design**: an inline font override was silently fighting the Nunito/Quicksand set up app-wide in `layout.tsx`, making these pages read as a different, less-finished product than the rest of MuniServe -- dropped, plus the near-invisible `0.5px` borders (~1.2:1 contrast) darkened to something from the real palette (~2.9:1). Applied to `/apply`, `/status/[reference]`, and `/verify/[reference]` alike.

**Verified**: `tsc`/`eslint`/a full `next build` clean, plus live browser verification of the critical fixes (label/input association, touch target sizing, the font/border change) rendered and confirmed directly, not just type-checked. Further verification (the deeper OTP-gated form screens specifically) was deliberately not pushed further than this to avoid spending a real SMS credit purely for the sake of testing.

---

## 7a9. Staff dashboard accessibility and design gaps fixed (2026-08-20, same audit)

The staff-side half of the same audit pass -- read via source code rather than a live login (staff pages need real Google OAuth, which can't be driven in this environment), the same fallback this project has used throughout its history for session-gated UI.

- **High, accessibility**: two mouse-only interactions -- Audit Trail's row expand and Permit History's column sort -- gained real keyboard support (focusable, `Enter`/`Space`-activatable) plus `aria-sort` on the sort headers.
- **High, accessibility**: five of the seven main staff pages (Applications, Department Review, Treasury, Mayor, Audit Trail, Stats) had no page-level heading at all for a screen reader to announce "what page am I on" -- added to all five (plus Businesses/Settings already had one).
- **Medium, design**: the four review-decision buttons (Approve / Approve with condition / Request info / Reject) were too visually similar -- only Approve was a solid filled button, and Request info/Approve with condition shared the same icon. Reject is now visually separated (a divider, bolder weight); Request info gets its own icon.
- **Medium, accessibility**: ~15 previously placeholder-only inputs across Settings, the payment queue, and the Business Registry (fee override amounts, GCash/bank account fields, OR numbers, ...) gained a real `aria-label`, so the field still has a name for a screen reader once someone starts typing and the placeholder disappears.
- **Medium, accessibility**: the Notes field's own "required if rejecting" claim was placeholder-only text, invisible again the instant someone started typing -- promoted to persistent helper text under the field (this was the audit's own fix for this finding; it did not yet make the requirement real, see section 7a12 below for the follow-up that actually did).
- **Medium, accessibility**: BPLO's clickable pipeline-stage tabs and the Audit Trail's view-toggle were real, focusable buttons already, but weren't marked up with actual ARIA tab roles -- added, so arrow-key navigation between them now works as a screen reader would expect.
- **Medium, design**: the Paused/Not-provisioned screens used raw hardcoded colors with no MuniServe branding at all, and ignored a staff member's dark-mode preference outright -- rebuilt on the shared design-token `Card` component.
- **Low, accessibility**: the sort-arrow and pagination glyphs (▲ ▼ ‹ ›) on Permit History gained real `aria-label`s, matching a pattern already used correctly elsewhere in this codebase (the barangay-list "remove" button).

**Verified**: `tsc`, `eslint`, and a full `next build` all clean.

---

## 7a10. Remaining low-severity audit findings closed (2026-08-20, same audit)

The last batch of low-priority items from the same pass, done as one small cohesive commit after the higher-severity work above:

- **Security headers**: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Strict-Transport-Security` added site-wide in `next.config.ts`, declared explicitly in both the default and the `/apply`-specific header blocks (rather than relying on assumed merge behavior between overlapping rules, since Next's own docs don't spell that out) -- verified live that `/apply` keeps its permissive `frame-ancestors` override for embeds while `/login` stays locked down, and both now carry the new headers.
- **Heading-case consistency**: `ApplyPausedNotice` was the one remaining Title Case heading on the applicant side, matched to sentence case everywhere else, plus given the same border-visibility fix already applied to the other applicant pages (section 7a8).
- **Dash-style consistency**: 9 instances of a plain `--` in Settings/Stats section subtitles normalized to a real em dash, matching the already-correct majority style in those same files.
- **Currency legibility**: a live formatted preview (e.g. "≈ ₱1,234,567") added under Capital Investment and Gross Sales on the applicant form, so a mistyped extra or missing zero is easier to catch before submitting.

**Deliberately left alone, flagged rather than silently folded in**: the "hardcoded colors instead of design tokens" finding across the applicant pages. Not a couple of spots -- most of `ApplyPageClient.tsx`'s few hundred inline style objects, and those pages are deliberately self-contained/single-theme by original design (no dark mode, not on the token system). Judged a large, real risk to a live government form for a purely cosmetic win if rushed into the same batch as everything else here -- worth its own dedicated pass if wanted, not this one.

**Verified**: `tsc`/`eslint`/a full `next build` clean, plus live-confirmed the new headers on both a public (`/apply`) and staff (`/login`) route. The currency-preview hint only renders on the OTP-gated form screen (can't be reached without a real SMS credit), so it was verified by calling the real, unmodified formatting function directly instead of a full click-through.
